NVIDIA Deepens Korean Sovereign AI Work on Chips and Rack Systems

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

NVIDIA announced on November 9, 2026 an expanded collaboration with Korea’s Ministry of Science and ICT spanning AI chips, data centers, models, skills and agent security. The hardware project centers on Rebellions’ R200 processors and NVLink-based rack systems, which are expected to be ready for deployment in 2028.

Under the joint announcement, Rebellions will connect its next-generation Rebel R200 neural processing units (NPUs) to NVIDIA’s platform through NVLink Fusion and use the MGX architecture for rack-scale system development. SK Telecom plans to deploy the systems in its AI data center, linking them to proprietary models and public-facing services under AI for All. MSIT is supporting the project as the start of broader work between NVIDIA and Korean NPU companies.

Rebellions describes the R200 as designed for low-latency, energy-efficient inference on frontier models, including models with multiple trillions of parameters. NVLink Fusion allows chipmakers to combine specialized silicon with NVIDIA’s high-bandwidth, low-latency scale-up networking and established infrastructure ecosystem.

The companies plan disaggregated inference, which assigns different stages of AI response generation to the processor architecture best suited to each task. NVIDIA GPUs would handle prefill and Rebellions NPUs decode, with the goal of improving inference throughput and power efficiency.

The MGX architecture provides a common rack footprint plus power-delivery and cooling elements that GPU and NPU systems can share. The shared design can support data center planning alongside Rebellions’ processor and system development, while the MGX ecosystem offers access to original design manufacturers and component suppliers.

Through AI for All, NVIDIA will work with three selected consortia and four Korean foundation-model builders, using Dynamo to optimize and scale inference. The accompanying deployment recipes cover SK Telecom’s A.X K2, LG AI Research’s K-EXAONE 2.0, Upstage’s Solar Open 2 and Motif Technologies’ Motif-3.

Dynamo is an open-source distributed inference-serving framework for multi-node deployments at data center scale. It coordinates request routing and communication when a model grows beyond one GPU and is split across multiple GPUs.

NVIDIA reports that the recipes, optimized for B200 GPUs, deliver up to 2x out-of-the-box performance. They are intended to help developers move the Korean open models into production and support broadly accessible AI for All services.

The workforce component adds NVIDIA training and certification programs, hackathons and technical seminars intended to strengthen GPU utilization. It extends the program beyond hardware and software into the operational skills needed to run GPU-based infrastructure.

On agent security, NVIDIA announced that Hyundai Motor Group is exploring NemoClaw blueprints and the OpenShell runtime. Korean security company AIM Intelligence is integrating its Starfort agent-control platform with OpenShell for employee-productivity and enterprise-workflow agents.

OpenShell, part of the NVIDIA Open Agent Safety Platform, provides sandboxed execution and policy-based controls for sensitive data, enterprise-tool access and autonomous agent actions. The Starfort integration is intended to combine those runtime access controls with detection of malicious instructions and data leakage. AIM Intelligence is also considering a preliminary proof of concept for the public energy sector.

The practical implication is a split between software and hardware readiness: model-serving recipes are being released for Korean developers, while the mixed GPU-NPU racks still require processor integration and system development before SK Telecom’s planned deployment.

The 500e Isn't Fiat's Smallest Car, But It Is The Only One You Can Drive On US Highways

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

It may be true that the smallest production cars ever built make modern compacts look huge, but everything's relative. The tiniest new car you can get for 2026 in the U.S. — the Fiat 500e — is still quite small when compared to most other rides today. Oh, and speaking of tiny, that word applies to the current Fiat lineup as well. It only includes the 500e and the adorable Fiat Topolino low-speed vehicle that was designed for stunting on your suburban neighbors – not traveling on U.S. highways.

The all-electric city car is a mere 142.95 inches long and 74.12 inches wide (including its mirrors), and its height is 66.3 inches. Note that the 2026 Mini Cooper is even more vertically challenged, as it checks in at 57.6 inches in height, yet it's nearly 16 inches longer and roughly 3.5 inches wider than the 500e. Meanwhile, for comparison's sake, one of the biggest new passenger vehicles available right now is another EV, the Cadillac Escalade IQL — a 750-horsepower, 19-foot-long American luxury beast. To be exact, the IQL stretches a tick over 19 feet, at 228.5 inches, and it's a whopping 94.1 inches wide.

The 500e's sticker price is also on the small side, at least compared to the iQL. The Fiat starts at $35,700 before a $1,995 destination charge, while the king-sized Cadillac starts at $130,405 and requires a destination charge of $2,895. Unfortunately for Fiat, the cost of the 500e doesn't look so good when you look at other small EVs like the Nissan Leaf (MSRP: $29,990) or even the Chevrolet Equinox (MSRP: $34,995).

All that said, you've got to hand it to Fiat engineers. They managed to carve out a surprising amount of interior space in the 500e's cabin in their efforts to help win America's heart. You'll find a total of 101.2 cubic feet of interior volume waiting there. True, only about 7.5 cubic feet is dedicated for rear storage behind the back seats. But the 93.7 cubic feet leftover for passengers is more than in some noticeably larger cars. For example, the 2026 Toyota Corolla only has 88.6 cubic feet of passenger space despite being almost 40 inches longer than the Fiat. The 2026 Hyundai Venue SUV checks in with 91.9 cubic feet of passenger space, even though it's roughly 16 inches longer than the 500e.

The Fiat's specs also translate into a real-world advantage. When Jalopnik's own Collin Woodard drove the 500e in 2024, he was still able to fit a 6-footer in the rear seats. Also noting the car's relatively meager 149-mile range, the bottom line was that the Fiat proved why city cars are best as EVs.

The flipside to the roomy interior is a standard cabin-equipment list that's a bit lacking, especially in a car that sets you back close to $38,000 as a starting point. Remember, the 2026 Nissan Leaf opens at $29,990, before $1,575 in destination charges, and it comes standard with blind-spot monitoring/intervention, rear cross-traffic alert, a driver-attention monitor, a surround-view system, a 12.3-inch touchscreen, and even Nissan's ProPILOT Assist driver-assist system — none of which is standard for the base 500e.

Rebellions to Use NVIDIA NVLink Fusion for Korean Sovereign AI

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

NVIDIA announced on November 8, 2026, a collaboration with Rebellions and SK Telecom to connect Rebellions’ next-generation R200 processors to NVLink Fusion for Korean sovereign AI infrastructure, with the resulting systems expected to be ready for deployment in 2028.

Under the agreement, Rebellions will link its Rebel R200 neural processing units (NPUs) through NVLink Fusion and develop rack-scale systems around the NVIDIA MGX architecture. SK Telecom plans to deploy those systems in its AI data center and connect them with its proprietary models and services. The announcement therefore establishes a development and deployment plan, rather than describing infrastructure already in operation.

Rebellions is developing the R200 with the goals of low-latency and energy-efficient inference using frontier models, including multitrillion-parameter mixture-of-experts models. These are design objectives for a processor still under development, not performance measurements from deployed systems.

NVLink Fusion extends NVIDIA’s AI platform to custom XPUs, allowing specialized processors to use NVLink scale-up networking designed for high bandwidth and low latency. Rebellions intends to use that networking to connect R200 NPUs within a rack, plugging its own silicon into NVIDIA’s wider infrastructure ecosystem.

The interconnect also supports a planned disaggregated-inference design, which assigns separate stages of AI response generation to different processor architectures. NVIDIA GPUs would handle the prefill stage and Rebellions NPUs the decode stage. The companies are targeting improved inference throughput and power efficiency from this arrangement, which keeps NVIDIA GPUs in the system while giving Rebellions’ NPUs a specialized inference role.

MGX addresses the physical infrastructure surrounding the processors. Its reference designs and ecosystem give Rebellions access to original design manufacturers and component suppliers. GPU and NPU systems can also share data center design elements such as rack footprint, power delivery and cooling, giving SK Telecom a basis for facilities planning while Rebellions continues its processor and system development.

SK Telecom’s contribution extends beyond housing the hardware. It plans to connect the infrastructure with proprietary foundation models, including A.X K2, and public-facing services developed under the Korean government’s AI for All project. The broader objective is to support AI services tailored to Korea’s language, industries and priorities, eventually establishing a foundation for services across the public and private sectors.

For SK Telecom, the resulting full-stack strategy spans data center operations, proprietary models and the services built on them. President and CEO Jung Jai-hun described combining those capabilities with Korean AI semiconductors and global infrastructure technology as a way to strengthen the company’s sovereign AI competitiveness.

Government backing is also part of the arrangement. Korea’s Ministry of Science and ICT is supporting collaboration between NVIDIA and Korean NPU companies, beginning with Rebellions and SK Telecom. Bae Kyung-hoon, Korea’s deputy prime minister and science and ICT minister, said it would enable Korean-developed NPUs to “enter the computing infrastructure market alongside NVIDIA” through NVLink Fusion. He also said the government would support tangible results and further cooperation with NVIDIA in areas including physical AI and securing GPU supply.

The processor collaboration follows a separate June 7, 2026, announcement outlining SK Telecom’s plan for a gigawatt-scale AI Cloud in Korea using NVIDIA’s DSX architecture. The first AI factory under that plan was scheduled to come online in 2027, and SK Telecom was set to become an NVIDIA Cloud Partner. The new processor work sits alongside that broader infrastructure relationship, with its own development and deployment schedule.

The plan gives Korean-developed silicon a defined route into NVIDIA-based AI factories, with SK Telecom providing the operating environment and connections to services. Before those services can use the R200, the companies must complete the processor and rack systems, integrate them into SK Telecom’s infrastructure and establish whether the proposed division of inference work delivers the intended efficiency gains.

Two Boy Meets World Stars Have A Spinoff Idea, And As A Fan, I Really Need It To Happen

Over a decade after Boy Meets World ended in 2000, a spinoff series was developed for Disney Channel and called Girl Meets World. But even after Girl ended in 2017, there were still some characters that fans wanted to see more of. That’s all the more reason I'm so excited to hear Will Friedle and Matthew Lawrence talking about their own spinoff idea. And, seriously, I need a network exec to hear them out!

Cory Matthews and Shawn Hunter may have been one of the most perfect best friend pairings, based on their exploits in Boy Meets World. However, those two characters' older brothers, Jack and Eric, are also truly a bromance deserving of attention. During a recent episode of Pod Meets World, Matthew Lawrence, who played Jack, said during a press junket that he was asked about a Jack/Eric spin-off series. Will Friedle dropped some very positive thoughts on the prospect, and they make me giddy:

I think you could still do it. Jack and Eric come together. It’s the new Odd Couple!

Yes! This needs to come together. For those who don’t know, Neil Simon’s play The Odd Couple is about two mismatched roommates, and Jack and Eric definitely fit that bill. While Jack is the logical straight man of the duo, Eric is the zany eccentric. As the two characters (and the actors who played) were so yin and yang, a spinoff would definitely make for some entertaining TV.

Lawrence went on to explain that a Jack/Eric spinoff was once in the cards, but the relationship between the creators and Disney “kind of fell apart.” Also, Lawrence recently told EW he could see the duo, both in their 40s, forced back together after going their separate ways. Lawrence continued to say on the podcast that if a potential spinoff isn’t picked up by television, he has an excellent idea of how a micro spin-off series could work, and I’m loving it:

If anything, it’s like the new YouTube in a sense that these days, as you see with Obsession and all of these things that are coming, I mean, that’s kind of the new groundlings when you talk about comedy. The new spot where they bring out shows. So maybe if we even start, maybe it’s like, build it and they will come.

It’s true that YouTube has become the new platform for creatives to bring their ideas to fruition before they hit the big or small screens. If there's enough attention, it would be so interesting to see if this possible BMW offshoot could hit streaming one day.

Friedle said himself he’s “totally down” for a spinoff, so I'm feeling somewhat hopeful that the two might try to see this through. The last time fans saw Jack’s big one-time return on Girl Meets World, he reunited with Eric. However, it was revealed that Jack was a high-ranking executive at an environmentally harmful firm, which clashed with Eric's position as a newly elected senator. However, the iconic duo mended fences at Eric’s niece’s school dance, with Jack admitting that his friendship with his old pal was “the best choice” he ever made.

This TV show idea seems better than a previously proposed spin-off idea that came while Boy Meets World was still airing. Friedle revealed he almost left the ABC/Disney sitcom when creator Michael Jacobs pitched a “young Friends” idea for Eric to move to L.A. and meet new friends there. While it’s always amusing to see Eric’s humorous interactions, I’d rather see a straight man/funny man dynamic between him and Jack.

Now that the idea of a Jack/Eric spinoff has been brought up, this Boy Meets World fan is keeping her fingers crossed that it comes to fruition. Given that mismatched roommate shows like The Big Bang Theory, 2 Broke Girls, Perfect Strangers have been successful, why not give Jack Hunter and Eric Matthews a shot?

In the meantime, fans can watch Eric and Jack's dynamic on Boy Meets World and Girl Meets World by streaming them with a Disney+ subscription.

Just your average South Floridian cinephile who believes the pen is mightier than the sword.

10 Apple products and features that transformed under Tim Cook's leadership

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Apple changed a lot in the last 15 years.

When Tim Cook became Apple's CEO in 2011, he began leading what was already one of the world's most successful technology companies. The iPhone was driving Apple's growth, the iPad was still relatively new and the Mac was at the center of its computing business. Cook inherited an enormous ecosystem, then he spent 15 years finding new ways it could be put to use.

By the end of Cook's tenure, Apple had introduced entirely new product categories, moved the Mac to its own processors, turned the iPhone into a wallet and satellite communicator and built a services business worth more than $100 billion a year. Meanwhile, some of Apple's biggest changes weren't new products, but rather familiar ones that slowly evolved into something else.

There are now more than 2.5 billion active Apple devices, while the company brought in $416.2 billion in net sales in fiscal 2025. Apple's announcement on Cook's transition lays out the scale of the company he leaves behind. But the more interesting story is how under his leadership, Apple products took on entirely new forms and jobs.

When the Apple Watch launched in 2015, it was primarily an iPhone companion for notifications, communication, apps and activity tracking. Apple began expanding its health and fitness capabilities with Apple Watch Series 2, which added built-in GPS, swim tracking and more workout features.

The bigger shift came with Apple Watch Series 4 in 2018. With this, Apple added an ECG app and fall detection, giving the Watch capabilities that went beyond basics like tracking steps. The ECG could record a heart rhythm directly from the wrist, while fall detection could automatically contact emergency services if the wearer appeared to have taken a hard fall and remained immobile.

The watchOS 11 update in 2024 added sleep apnea notifications, the Vitals app and Training Load, giving you more ways to track and interpret health and fitness data. In 2025, the Apple Watch Series 11 introduced sleep scoring and hypertension notifications. While the watch started as an accessory for your iPhone, it now monitors a whole suite of metrics that the iPhone can't.

AirPods could have been just a pair of wireless earbuds for your iPhone, but Apple kept giving them more reasons to exist within the larger Apple ecosystem. Automatic pairing, seamless switching between devices, active noise cancellation and spatial audio made them an attractive option for all your music and other audio.

Apple has given AirPods an important role beyond entertainment, though. Starting with AirPods Pro 2, Apple added a scientifically validated Hearing Test, Hearing Aid functionality for people with mild to moderate hearing loss and Hearing Protection. These hearing health tools make support more accessible to lots of people. Meanwhile, the AirPods Pro 3 added heart rate sensing to track your workout effort with the buds you're likely already wearing while exercising.

Under Cook, Apple took a familiar product and expanded what it could do, without asking people to adopt an entirely new device.

For years, Apple built its own chips for the iPhone, iPad and Apple Watch while relying on Intel processors for the Mac. That changed in 2020, when Apple announced a two-year transition to its own silicon. The move gave Mac computers a common architecture with Apple's other products, providing developers with a more unified platform for building software across the ecosystem.

The first Apple silicon Macs arrived later that year. Apple put the M1 chip in the MacBook Air, 13-inch MacBook Pro and Mac mini, combining the CPU, GPU, Neural Engine and other components into a single system-on-a-chip. This helped Apple push the Mac toward the same performance and power efficiency that had come to define its mobile devices.

The difference between Apple's processors and Intel's isn't just speed. After Apple took over this core piece of Mac hardware, it had holistic control of the hardware design, chips inside and macOS itself. This helped the Mac overcome its midlife crisis.

What started as a convenient way to snap a picture became a sophisticated camera system with multiple lenses, computational photography, Night mode and some serious video capabilities.

The iPhone 11 was a big turning point. It featured an Ultra Wide camera, Night mode and Deep Fusion, which uses machine learning to process photos pixel by pixel for more detail and less noise. The iPhone 12 Pro then added Apple ProRAW, combining Apple's computational photography with the flexibility of a RAW file for those who wanted more control over their images.

Apple's more recent iPhones have continued this progress. The iPhone 15 Pro brought a 48MP main camera, multiple focal-length options, 48MP ProRAW and professional video features including ProRes recording to external storage. Our iPhone 15 review highlighted the camera as a major part of the upgrade. Later, our iPhone 17 review covered more camera improvements, including a new Center Stage front camera and upgraded rear cameras. For many people, their iPhone camera is the way they take pictures, and what all other smartphone cameras are judged against.

Apple Pay is easy to take for granted because it feels so normal now. But in 2014, putting a payment system directly into the iPhone people already carried everywhere gave the device a new role: replacing the cards in their wallet. Apple Pay eventually expanded beyond in-store purchases to apps, websites and other Apple devices like the Apple Watch.

10 years after its launch, the company stated that Apple Pay was used by hundreds of millions of people across 78 regions, at millions of websites and apps and tens of millions of stores. It was also supported by more than 11,000 banks and "network partners" at that milestone. Apple Pay later expanded to third-party iOS and desktop browsers, providing more ways to check out online without typing your card details into every site.

That idea of leaving your physical wallet behind has grown beyond payments, too. Wallet can hold other items like event tickets, transit cards, keys and even eligible IDs. In 2025, Apple started expanding its Digital ID for use with participating US states, plus support for Japan's My Number Card. Expanded Apple Wallet ticket features have also added venue maps, parking directions and other event information.

Some of the iPhone's biggest changes under Cook had more to do with connectivity than the phone itself, as Apple kept expanding the ways your iPhone can connect to the world around it. The iPhone 12 brought 5G to Apple's phones in 2020, while MagSafe introduced a ring of magnets that made wireless charging easier and created an all-new suite of snap-on accessories.

The iPhone 14 added something even more ambitious: direct satellite connections to message emergency services when cellular and Wi-Fi coverage isn't available. Apple also added the ability to share a location through Find My via satellite. In 2024, satellite connectivity was expanded to support Messages via satellite for messaging friends when you have no other options.

Not everything that changed under Tim Cook came in a box. Apple's services business grew from a relatively small part of the company into a $109.2 billion business, making it a core part of Apple's revenue alongside the iPhone.

The category is broader than just subscriptions. It includes the App Store, advertising, cloud services, Apple Music, Apple TV, Apple Pay and more. In 2025, the App Store alone averaged more than 850 million weekly users, while Apple said developers had earned more than $550 billion through the platform since 2008. Apple's services revenue also hit a quarterly record in early 2026, topping $30 billion.

Hardware still accounts for most of Apple's revenue, but the company's various services provide a huge business built around what people do after they buy an Apple device.

Apple had been selling digital content long before the Apple TV streaming service (formerly called Apple TV+) arrived in 2019. But with it, the company was no longer just offering access to content made by others. Apple TV+ launched with an original catalog of shows, movies and documentaries, including The Morning Show, See and For All Mankind.

Apple quickly built a reputation around those originals. CODA became the first streaming film to win the Academy Award for Best Picture in 2022, while shows like Ted Lasso and Severance became some of the service's biggest hits.

Apple TV+ earned a record 81 Emmy nominations across 14 Apple Original titles in 2025, led by Severance with 27 nominations and The Studio with 23. Both shows also took home awards that year. By September 2025, Apple said its originals had accumulated 620 wins and 2,816 award nominations.

Vision Pro is different from almost everything else Apple released under Cook. Instead of putting another screen in front of you, Apple built a computer that adds your digital life into the space around you. When Apple unveiled Vision Pro in 2023, it called the device its first "spatial computer," with visionOS and an interface controlled by your eyes, hands and voice.

What may be more interesting than the $3,499 headset is where Apple thinks computing could go next. Apps can float around a room instead of being confined to a traditional display, which provides immersive environments with three-dimensional content. More than 600 apps and games built specifically for Vision Pro were available when it launched.

As our M5 Vision Pro review found, the device is unlikely to become the next big Apple platform unless future revisions make it more accessible. But that's why it's worth a mention here: Vision Pro is Apple trying to create a new platform instead of solely relying on what's worked in the past.

The premise of an AirTag is simple: attach one to something you don't want to lose and your iPhone will help you find it. Its bigger innovation is how the tracker taps into Apple's existing Find My network, which uses nearby Apple devices to detect an AirTag and anonymously report its approximate location to its owner. An AirTag doesn't need its own cellular connection or GPS to be useful.

Apple describes AirTag as "the bestselling item finder", and continues expanding what it can do. The second-generation AirTag, released in 2026, added a longer Bluetooth range and more precise finding. Apple's Share Item Location feature also lets you securely share an item's location with participating airlines to help recover lost luggage.

The second-generation AirTag's primary usefulness comes from Apple's enormous network of devices, giving it access to a vast web to help locate a lost item.

Behrami: "Sembra assurdo ma l'Inter sente più la rivalità col Como che col Milan"

Intervenuto ai microfoni di Dazn, Valon Behrami ha parlato così di Inter dopo la vittoria perentoria contro il Como a San Siro:

"L'Inter viene stimolata da questa sfida col Como, del Como si parla bene, tutti parlano di Fabregas e questa sfida col Como accende particolarmente l'Inter. Di Canio: "Inter ingiocabile quando sta così, è solo questione di testa. Spence? In Italia gioca bendato"

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Di Canio: "Inter ingiocabile quando sta così, è solo questione di testa. Spence? In Italia gioca bendato"

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Paolo Di Canio, dagli studi di Sky Sport, ha analizzato la vittoria dell'Inter contro il Como: "Lautaro se manca è un fattore, è un leader, anche al 60% soprattutto in certe partite ha la qualità tecnica per liberarsi, sa calciare come pochi... Però Thuram sta facendo la differenza: anche nelle ultime partite, quando l'Inter non produceva, lui si muoveva, si sbatteva, andava a cercare la palla, svariava su tutto il fronte d'attacco. È la squadra che cambia, e oggi abbiamo visto la vera Inter".

"La cosa buona dell'Inter è che ci sono degli anticorpi in società per cui non possono rovinare quello che si sono costruiti in questi 6 anni: assorbono quello che è il dettame della società, dell'ambiente serio. Si può perdere un attimo la retta via perchè ci siamo imborghesiti, perchè sembra tutto facile, ma quando non ti riescono più le cose? Non siamo più quelli? Le prime 6 partite abbiamo parlato di dominio, di Inter ingiocabile, che è quello che penso io quando la vedo come oggi. Se si perdono, poi si ritrovano perchè c'è un ambiente sano e serio. Gli interventi di Akanji sono stati con una gamba tonica, e hanno giocato in settimana: è solo una questione di testa, al netto di qualche difetto che può avere come tante altre squadre".

"Queste freschezza, questa reattività, non è frutto di casualità, così come non era casualità quando prendeva gol. Quell'approccio era un difetto: lenti, compassati, di testa, nel giro palla, recuperi con i tempi sbagliati. Questa è l'Inter, che se ti aggredisce, ti viene a prendere, non è che le altre sono fenomenali a difendere. Il cambio di modulo contro il Milan mi è sembrata una scelta scriteriata, fai perdere identità alla squadra: succede anche ai migliori allenatori del mondo. Diouf, un mancino, che è una mezz'ala dribblomane, che si mette a fare il quinto, disattento, che non ha il destro naturale... Spence? Gioca con una benda in Italia... con la benda gioca. Ma questo è stato titolare al Mondiale con la terza Nazionale del mondo. Non è che parliamo di uno... ha una gamba, una tecnica. La palla che dà, è un assist da Premier: palla violenta, o fai autogol o segna il mio compagno. Ma la capacità tecnica anche nell'uscita ce l'ha. Poi a volte si addormenterà perché è un po' così, ma se gli tolgono quel difetto sarà più facile che faccia lui il quinto con quell'atletismo, quella qualità e quella tecnica".

Ramayana Producer On How Being A ‘Fanboy’ Of Chris Nolan Films Inspired One Thing About The Epic

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Forget Dunesday, Jack Black Would Like To Get Jumanji In On The Christmas Movie Nickname Fun

Someone Asked Timothèe Chalamet How He Feels About Dune’s Ending (And Now I Can't Wait)

Someone Finally Asked Matt Damon When He Knew The Odyssey Would Be A Hit. His Answer Was Really Satisfying

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

The Indian movie features one awesome collab.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

India’s most expensive movie ever is Ramayana, which is one of the latest 2026 movie releases to hit theaters. The movie was a massive undertaking, and that's evident just by watching the trailer. All in all, the film is firmly steeped in the culture its representing. But, even though the flick was made thousands of miles from Hollywood, a producer was very much inspired by one of our greatest filmmakers, Christopher Nolan, as CinemaBlend recently learned.

Did you know that Ramayana is co-composed by Hans Zimmer? The German musician has become one of the most beloved and prolific composers of modern cinema, and that’s exactly why the movie's producer, Namit Malhotra, asked for him to come aboard the filmmaking team. Here’s how he told us the story during CB’s exclusive Ramayana interview:

I grew up in India completely fanboying on A.R. Rahman, who I absolutely love and his music has sort of formed a big part of my growing up and childhood and youth. And then, I moved to L.A. in 2010, the year Inception came out. I started to fall in love with Chris Nolan and Han Zimmer's music that was done for The Dark Knight and all the films that came subsequent to that. And, it was one of those moments when we decided to make Ramayana. I said literally as a fanboy, ‘What if I could take Han Zimmer and A.R. Rahman together and they would create a new sound, a new feeling, a new energy that the world has not heard before?’ And everybody is like, ‘Yeah, why not?’

Malhotra called himself a self-described “fanboy” of Nolan movies who's been in awe of his movies for a while. So, when he went on to make his own epic, he thought he'd take the long-shot move to try to get Zimmer on board for Ramayana alongside his favorite composer from India, A.R. Rahman. (Did you know Zimmer actually helped convince Nolan to make Interstellar, by the way? A greatly overlooked detail is just how important musicians like him are pivotal to the filmmaking process.) As Malhotra added:

One thing I learned in America, there's no harm in asking because the worst you'll be told is ‘No’. That's not how we grew up in India. Like you normally don't ask the question if you think the answer is no.

Even though Namit Malhotra had never met Zimmer and didn't know would react to the request, the producer took “a cue from [his] American friends” and reached out to the Interstellar composer. This is what happened next:

I made a pitch and a presentation to him. I couldn't have been more surprised by his intellect, his intuitive judgment because he was like, ‘Namit, you don't have to sell me on Ramayana. If something has existed in the world for over 5,000 years and generation after generation, more people are excited to consume it, learn from it, and it's still not lost its relevance, there must be more than what you and I can understand. So we should just figure out how we do this and not worry about whether we should do it.’

Ramayana Is Making A Huge Global Push. I Still Can't Tell From Reviews What I'm Getting Into

The idea really does sell itself, doesn’t it? Ramayana is a Hindu epic poem that’s very central to India’s culture. It has inspired the celebrations of Diwali and Dussehra, and the story also serves as a moral cornerstone as well. It focuses on two characters: Rama and Ravana. Prince Rama is the seventh incarnation / avatar of Lord Vishnu, who becomes responsible for taking down King Ravana from having uncontrollable power over people. It's India's own classic good vs. evil story, and it has really stood the test of time.

I'm no sure how aware Zimmer was of the legend of Ramayana before it was pitched to him, yet he was apparently sold quite quickly. He got the importance of it being told for the big screen. Malhotra told us that Zimmer’s reaction to his pitch even “revalidated” his own reasons to make it and “reinvigorated [his] own confidence” since he respects the composer so much.

Hans Zimmer is a busy musical savant, who’s recently worked on the Dune movies and the upcoming Harry Potter TV series, so he could have easily passed, but he clearly didn’t want to. And, as a result of his participation, one of Hollywood's best got to put his mark on a historical Indian film. You can hear Zimmer’s original music for Ramayana, which is playing in theaters around the world right now.

In college, she was the Managing Editor of the award-winning college paper, The Daily Titan, where she specialized in writing/editing long-form features, profiles and arts & entertainment coverage, including her first run-in with movie reporting, with a phone interview with Guillermo del Toro for Best Picture winner, The Shape of Water. Now she's into covering YA television and movies, and plenty of horror. Word webslinger. All her writing should be read in Sarah Connor’s Terminator 2 voice over.

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang starring in possible remake of The Spy Who Shagged Me

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Admits to affair after company board opens probe into alleged contact with possible Chinese asset who bought GPUs in bulk

Nvidia’s board has opened an investigation into its CEO Jensen Huang after he admitted to an affair, amid allegations he had a romantic relationship with a woman US authorities feel is likely a Chinese spy.

The AI hardware giant’s board published a statement on Sunday afternoon that states it is “initiating an independent investigation in light of recent reporting that founder and CEO Jensen Huang has been the target of a foreign intelligence effort.”

The New York Times is the source of that reporting. On October 29 it alleged US government intelligence agencies decided to investigate a woman who goes by Alice Huang and/or Huang Le, on grounds she passes intelligence with Beijing.

In that story The Times used the term “close relationship” to describe the Nvidia CEO's associations with both Ms Huang and US president Donald Trump.

On Sunday the newspaper alleged US authorities believe Alice Huang and Jensen Huang shared a "romantic" relationship.

The Times further alleges Ms Huang is a Chinese citizen who worked for a Singapore-based company called Megaspeed that has bought plenty of Nvidia hardware and is suspected of doing so to make it available to Chinese users, either by running inside datacenters or perhaps even facilitating transport to the Middle Kingdom.

The US has for years banned or restricted exports of advanced GPUs to China, partly because it feels Beijing’s armed forces can use them to build systems that will erode America’s military superiority.

Any relationship, of any sort, between Jensen Huang and a Chinese intelligence operative would therefore be enormously concerning. The company told the NYT that Jensen Huang and Alice Huang just had normal CEO-to-customer-exec chats that were no threat to Nvidia intellectual property or US national security.

Shortly after Nvidia’s board published its statement, Huang sent an email to staff that the company made public.

“I owe my family and all of you an apology,” the email opens. “I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did. I am deeply sorry.”

Huang’s email doesn’t explicitly link his actions to The Times’ reports, but he says he supports the Nvidia board’s decision to open its investigation “and will cooperate fully.”

"To my colleagues at NVIDIA, including those I have worked alongside for decades, and to our board, partners, and shareholders around the world: you deserve better from me," he wrote, without specifying if that's a reference to infidelity. "Leading NVIDIA is the privilege of my life. My responsibility is to serve you and be worthy of your trust. I have fallen short of that responsibility. I am fully committed to NVIDIA and will work to earn back your confidence."

“I love our country and our company,” the CEO added. “I am committed to protecting NVIDIA and supporting America’s national security. I am proud and grateful to be an American.”

The CEO’s mail, and the board’s statement, make no allegations about any “close relationship” with Ms. Huang.

The exact nature of that relationship therefore remains mysterious, as does Nvidia’s relationship with Singapore, a tiny island nation that has sometimes been the source of 22 percent of the company’s revenue.

Singapore is simply incapable of hosting as much Nvidia kit as that volume of purchases would buy, because its government limits datacenters to 1.6 GW of energy consumption. When asked to explain that oddity, Nvidia has said that it’s satisfied Singaporean entities buy its hardware on behalf of end-users in other locations, and that such deals are not used to evade the USA’s sanctions.

Nvidia is not a notably scandal-prone company, but in 2022 it did admit to having previously misrepresented the impact of cryptocurrency mining on sales of its gaming hardware. China has opened an antitrust case, and US authorities may have considered doing likewise.

Jensen Huang, however, has never presented as the kind of CEO who makes operating at the edge of the law a regular tactic. Nor does he hold sufficient Nvidia shares or voting power to unilaterally make major decisions.

This leaves the leader of the world’s top AI company appearing to have made a very human error. ®

The Southwest Rapid Rewards® Priority Credit Card (See rates and fees) offers 90,000 bonus points after spending $3,000 on purchases in the first 3 months from account opening. It has a $229 annual fee. The Southwest Rapid Rewards® Plus Credit Card (See rates and fees) offers 80,000 bonus points after spending $1,000 on purchases in the first 3 months from account opening. Its annual fee is $99. That’s a much lower spending requirement for a bonus that’s only 10,000 points smaller. Southwest’s Companion Pass lets someone fly with you for just taxes and fees, even when you redeem points for your own ticket. For someone who flies Southwest with a partner a few times a year, it’s the single best deal in domestic air travel. And the easiest way to earn it is about to disappear. There’s still a final window to use an 80,000- or 90,000-point credit card initial bonus toward a pass valid through December 31, 2027. You need to apply by December 2, 2026 and get approved. I’d choose Plus for the lower cost of getting the bonus. Priority costs $130 more per year, but gives you better seat benefits, 4,500 more anniversary points and a larger initial bonus. For a family that flies Southwest regularly, those seating benefits can easily be worth the difference. Bilt Palladium Card (See rates and fees) has a strong offer of $300 in Bilt Cash on approval plus 50,000 points after $4,000 in non-housing spend in the first 3 months. When you earn the initial bonus you also receive Bilt Rewards Gold elite status valid for the rest of the year and the entire next year. That unlocks early access to events and special activities, higher transfer bonuses, and preserves 1:1 transfers from Rakuten (they’ve only promised that for all members for six months). It also unlocks access to Bilt’s Home Away From Home program which you might think of as similar to American Express Fine Hotels & Resorts for their Platinum cardmembers. Palladium comes with a Priority Pass that includes two complimentary guests. The card earns 2 Bilt Points per dollar on everyday spend. And you can choose to earn 4% back in Bilt Cash as well, which unlocks point earnings on rent and mortgage payments with no transaction fee (as above, spending 75% of your housing payment lets you earn as many additional points as you spend on rent and mortgage). And you can redeem Bilt Cash to earn an additional 1 point per dollar spent up to $5,000 in spend, up to five times per year. Up to $100 of Bilt Cash earned rolls over to the next year. That means you can earn 3.3 points per dollar spent up to the amount of your housing payment, and 3 points per dollar spent on up to $25,000 after that. And I consider Bilt to have the most valuable points, with the most and best points transfer partners and the ability to redeem points at 1.25 cents apiece against the cost of travel through their portal. It also gives you enough elite nights to choose a confirmable suite upgrade without first earning those nights through hotel stays. "Il Como è diventata una squadra europea, gioca un calcio europeo ma per una squadra di qualità come l'Inter è più semplice giocare contro una squadra europea come il Como che una squadra che ti sporca il gioco, è questo più la qualità dei singoli e la forza fisica che hanno fatto la differenza. Shiba Inu (SHIB), Tron (TRX), Zcash (ZEC) and Near Protocol (NEAR) Price Analysis For November 9: From the Market Fear to Neutrality

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

From a technical standpoint, SHIB has moved further into a downtrend.

According to the technical analysis, the price is struggling to hold the important psychological threshold: $0.00000500 has already been broken, and SHIB now trades at approximately $0.00000447.

As for the future, SHIB is clearly moving in a longer-term downtrend; however, a local bounce toward $0.00000500 is possible. On the daily chart, the 200 moving average is at approximately $0.00000545.

Key price levels for SHIB are: $0.00000440 as the closest psychological support level, $0.00000410 as the next support, and 0.00000500–0.00000530 as the first psychological resistance level.

From the momentum standpoint, RSI is moving toward an oversold level. A break below the $0.00000410 support level could trigger a further decline, with a possible acceleration toward $0.00000400.

According to Shibarium data, DeFi liquidity is getting thinner, and TVL (Total Value Locked) fell to $41,000 in late October. Such a volume is irrelevant for the price of relatively large assets like Shiba Inu, which require millions to move.

Token burning stays down too. According to ShibBurn service data, only 375 million SHIB were burned in the last 30 days. With almost 600 trillion tokens in circulation, it is a drop in the ocean for Shiba Inu.

TRON is now trading at $0.3207, below the 200 moving average on the daily chart. At the end of September 2026, the supply of USDT and USDD on TRON was $93,880,000,000.

From a technical standpoint, on the daily TRX/USDT chart, the price has bounced somewhat from a local psychological support level of approximately $0.300. RSI remains below the neutral 50 level.

Key price levels for TRX are: $0.315 as the closest psychological support level, $0.305 as the next support, and $0.335 as the first psychological resistance level. On the daily chart, the 200 moving average is at approximately $0.328.

A break above $0.330 could put the price in recovery mode, opening the way to the $0.345 zone.

Based on data from TRON DAO's "Moving Trillions, Empowering Billions" research, the blockchain has already processed $2.2 trillion worth of stablecoins (settlement volume). The supply of USDT and USDD is at $93 billion, and total value locked crossed $13 billion.

According to data from explorers, network utilization stays above 1.92 million USDT transfers. Demand rose while the TRX price declined.

In September, according to Canary's official statement, a Staked TRX ETF was launched. It gives institutional investors and companies exposure to the asset, and they can now add TRX-related products to their balance sheets.

Zcash (ZEC) is currently trading at $1,272, moving sideways. From a longer-term standpoint, the overall trend is still bullish, despite the price weakness.

On the daily chart, key price levels for ZEC are: $1,220 as the 50 moving average, $1,080 as the 100 moving average, and $1,350 as the first psychological resistance level.

The Zcash network activated the NU7 update on November 6, a strong catalyst for the fundamental health of the network that cuts blockchain time from 50 to 25 seconds. It brings transaction confirmation speed to the next level, while rewarding ZEC mining activity even more.

RSI remains near the neutral 50 level. A break above $1,350 could put the price in recovery mode, opening the way to the $1,450 zone.

Considering the behavior of the market, a break below the $1,200 support level could trigger a further decline, with a possible acceleration toward $1,100.

From a technical standpoint, NEAR has moved further into a downtrend. NEAR Protocol is currently trading at $3.71. On the daily chart, the 50 and 100 moving averages are at approximately $4.25 and $4.65, and the 200 moving average is at approximately $3.65. RSI remains below the neutral 50 level.

NEAR Intents has seen more than $33,000,000,000 in volume.

Key price levels for NEAR are: $3.60 as a support level, $3.30 as the next support, and $4.65 as resistance.

More selling pressure could mean a further decline toward $3.00. A break above $4.20 could put the price in recovery mode.

We Tracked The World's Longest Airbus A321XLR Flight: Here's The Detour It Takes Every Day

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

On October 25, IndiGo started operating its flights between Mumbai Airport (BOM) and Amsterdam Schiphol Airport(AMS) with the Airbus A321XLR, replacing the leased Boeing 787-9 Dreamliners previously used on the route. At approximately 3,707 nautical miles (6,865 km), this is now the world's longest scheduled route operated by the A321XLR, with a scheduled flight time of around 10 hours from India to the Netherlands.

However, the actual distance flown is considerably longer due to airspace restrictions across the Middle East and South Asia. Using the Simple Flying Flight Tracker, we examined the routing of flight 22 from Amsterdam to Mumbai on November 1, as well as the flight times recorded during the aircraft's first 11 days on the route. Interestingly, the data shows that although the A321XLR is smaller and generally slower than the Boeing 787-9 Dreamliner, its average flight time to Amsterdam has been shorter.

On November 1, IndiGo flight 22 departed Amsterdam at 2:31 PM local time, operated by an A321XLR registered VT-NLC. According to our flight tracking data, the aircraft spent approximately nine hours and nine minutes in the air before landing in Mumbai at 4:10 AM local time the following morning. Rather than following the shortest possible route toward India, the aircraft followed a more southern trajectory through Europe and the Middle East.

After departing Amsterdam, the aircraft headed southeast across Central Europe before continuing toward Turkey and Iraq. From there, the route passed through the Gulf region and Oman, avoiding Iranian airspace, before continuing toward India's western coast and eventually Mumbai. The tracking data shows how the aircraft avoids both Pakistan and Iran, despite these countries lying along the more direct route between Mumbai and Amsterdam.

The distance between Mumbai and Amsterdam is about 3,707 nautical miles (6,865 km) along the shortest route. However, according to Aerospace Global News, the necessary detours can increase the actual distance flown to approximately 4,200 nautical miles (7,778 km), or around 13% longer. This puts the flight much closer to the A321XLR's advertised maximum range of 4,700 nautical miles (8,704 km).

IndiGo's 8-hour Bali flights showcase the A321XLR's potential to transform aviation route planning as we know it.

One of the main reasons for IndiGo's detour is the ongoing closure of Pakistani airspace to Indian airlines, in place since April 2025 following rising tensions between the two countries. According to the International Civil Aviation Organization (ICAO), the restrictions apply to aircraft registered in India, as well as those owned, operated, or leased by Indian carriers. Pakistan has since extended the restrictions several times.

At the same time, the ongoing conflict in the Middle East has also made flying through Iran increasingly difficult. Although Iranian airspace is not subject to the same ban for Indian airlines, the European Union Aviation Safety Agency (EASA) currently advises operators to avoid Iranian airspace at all altitudes.

For IndiGo, Pakistan's restrictions and the security situation in the Middle East considerably reduce available routing options between India and Europe. The airline must therefore follow a longer route, which increases fuel requirements and operating costs. The implications are particularly significant for the A321XLR, as the aircraft has far less flexibility to carry additional fuel and passengers on such a long flight than a widebody such as the Boeing 787-9 previously used on the route.

We tracked a Widerøe Dash 8 through 17 sectors in one day. The shortest flight lasted seven minutes.

Although Airbus advertises the A321XLR with a range of up to 4,700 nautical miles (8,704 km), the actual range depends on several operational factors, including passenger weight, cargo, fuel reserves, and weather conditions. The additional distance flown between Mumbai and Amsterdam consequently puts the aircraft much closer to its operational limits. To ensure that the flight can be completed nonstop, IndiGo reportedly restricts the number of seats it makes available for sale.

According to avicionline, IndiGo is currently selling fewer than 140 seats on the Mumbai-Amsterdam leg, despite its A321XLRs having a total capacity of 195 passengers. The aircraft are configured with 12 IndiGoStretch business class seats and another 183 economy class seats, meaning that over 25% of capacity is reportedly unavailable for sale. However, this restriction is not necessarily identical in both directions, as the aircraft's fuel requirements may differ depending on weather and other operating conditions.

Compared with the 787-9 previously operated on the route, this represents a considerable reduction in available capacity. The Norse Atlantic Airways aircraft offered 338 seats, including 56 premium and 282 economy class seats, according to IndiGo's original route announcement. While the A321XLR already has 143 fewer installed seats, additional restrictions on the Mumbai-Amsterdam leg mean almost 200 fewer seats may be available than on the previous widebody operation.

Interestingly, analysis of Flight Tracker data indicates that in the first 11 days of A321XLR operations, the aircraft spent less time in the air on flights from Mumbai to Amsterdam than the Boeing 787-9. Between October 25 and November 4, flight 6E21 recorded an average airborne time of nine hours and 36 minutes, approximately 20 minutes shorter than the average of nine hours and 56 minutes recorded by the 787-9 during its final nine flights. However, the opposite was true in the other direction: the A321XLR averaged nine hours to fly from Amsterdam to Mumbai, compared with eight hours and 31 minutes for the 787-9.

Looking at the individual flights, the fastest A321XLR service from Mumbai to Amsterdam was recorded on October 28, when VT-NLD completed the journey in nine hours and 19 minutes. The longest was the first flight on October 25, when the same aircraft spent nine hours and 52 minutes in the air. For comparison, the longest 787-9 flight during the period examined took 10 hours and 15 minutes on October 20. On the return journey, the fastest A321XLR service took eight hours and 32 minutes, while the longest required nine hours and 14 minutes, compared with a range of eight hours and 19 minutes to eight hours and 43 minutes for the 787-9.

However, this does not necessarily mean the A321XLR is operating more efficiently or at higher speeds than the 787-9. The two aircraft were operating at different times of day, with the 787-9 previously scheduled to depart Mumbai at 12:25 PM and Amsterdam at 8:35 PM local time, compared with 6:25 AM and 2:15 PM for the A321XLR. The comparison also covers two different periods around the start of the winter schedule, meaning wind conditions, air traffic restrictions, and the actual routings flown may have differed. The 787-9 also generally cruises faster than the A321XLR, making it difficult to attribute differences in airborne time to the aircraft types themselves.

Ultra-long-haul flying keeps pushing further. Singapore Airlines’ nonstop service between Singapore and New York is one of the longest commercial flights in the world, and Qantas’ Project Sunrise aims to link Sydney with London and New York nonstop using specially configured Airbus A350-1000s. Other marathons include Qantas’ Perth to London route and Air New Zealand’s Auckland to New York flights. Whether you were on board for 18 hours or followed a long-haul journey on the Simple Flying Flight Tracker, tell us the longest flight you’ve taken or tracked, and what made it memorable.

According to flight tracking data, IndiGo has already used four different Airbus A321XLRs on its Mumbai–Amsterdam service since October 25. In addition to VT-NLD, this includes VT-NLA, VT-NLB, and VT-NLC, with all four operating at least two flights from Mumbai to Amsterdam during the first 11 days of operations. VT-NLC has been the most frequently used aircraft, completing four services in each direction, while VT-NLD operated three flights to Amsterdam and VT-NLA and VT-NLB operated two each.

Although four aircraft appear in the flight-tracking data, the schedule allows IndiGo to operate both directions with the same aircraft within about 24 hours. On November 1, for example, VT-NLC landed in Amsterdam shortly before noon local time, before departing again at 2:31 PM. This left approximately two hours and 33 minutes between landing and takeoff, after which the aircraft arrived back in Mumbai at 4:10 AM local time the following morning.

The relatively short turnaround times allow IndiGo to operate the daily service without dedicating several aircraft exclusively to the route. Meanwhile, the airline has continued rotating its A321XLRs across other international services, including flights to Athens and Istanbul Airport (IST), where the aircraft type is also being deployed. The Mumbai-Amsterdam route nevertheless represents a considerably longer operation than those services, making it particularly dependent on the aircraft's available range and the routing restrictions currently affecting flights between India and Europe.

Sega’s Most Influential Platformer Deserves Its Big Remake Moment Next

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

With The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time and even Star Fox recently proving there is still an appetite for remakes done right, other game publishers are likely looking at their backlogs for inspiration. Sega, no stranger to fantastic franchises, suddenly finds itself once more in an enviable position. It has a bunch of great old games ripe for revival, and several of them seem capable of making a mark again.

One franchise that deserves to make its triumphant return celebrates its 25th anniversary this month. After its arcade launch earlier that year, Super Monkey Ball arrived as a GameCube launch game in November 2001. With proper care, that simple but effective game could thrive in 2026, even though recent entries in the series haven’t made quite the same splash.

Super Monkey Ball’s main event lets players choose one out of four monkeys. The simian is then enclosed in a ball that resembles the capsules we used to collect from toy machines at the supermarket as children. From there, they roll toward goal posts on floating platforms, collecting bananas along the way to improve their score. If they fall off the platform or run out of time before reaching their destination, they lose a life.

In the traditional sense of the term, Super Monkey Ball isn’t a platformer. There’s no sign of Mario, for instance, and jumping isn’t a central mechanic. But there are platforms and pits, so the label fits in a more general sense.

When Super Monkey Ball debuted, it reminded me immediately of an old NES favorite of mine, Marble Madness. But that pioneering older game could be completed in five minutes. There were only a few stages, all of them far too short for my liking. I wanted more of that sort of experience, which Super Monkey Ball delivered. There are dozens of stages. Most of them are smaller than the typical Marble Madness level, but they’re similarly compelling, and their quantity makes up for their brevity.

At the start of Super Monkey Ball, rolling through the goal tape is easy enough. Even by the end of that first set of courses, however, the process grows tense. Then there are 30 more courses of more difficult vintage to tackle, and an even larger and more demanding batch after that.

As they advance, players control not the monkeys themselves but the platforms they occupy. Mastery requires the player to tilt each board, which in turn allows the balls to build up enough speed to launch from small bumps in order to clear gaps, or to negotiate rougher terrain, all without moving so quickly that it becomes impossible to proceed along razor-thin beams that span wide gaps.

A full-fledged remake of that original game could add visual polish. It might also double down on the personality of the starring characters, who make irresistible mascots. AiAi even drove a cart in 2012’s Sonic & All-Stars Racing Transformed, besides starring in various Super Monkey Ball sequels. The included party games and competitive play, which feature attractions such as launching balls toward distant targets, could provide satisfying online play with friends or strangers, especially if they were expanded with additional content.

Sega certainly hasn’t forgotten about Super Monkey Ball. In fact, Super Monkey Ball Triple Pack, a physical-only bundle for Nintendo Switch, will be released on Nov. 10. That compilation is welcome, gathering several recent installments in the series, like Banana Rumble. It also includes Banana Mania, which remastered the stages from the original GameCube releases. But none of those modern installments have managed to have quite the impact that the original one once did, and might again if it were to receive an ambitious remake like Nintendo produced with Ocarina of Time. The main question now is whether Sega has the desire to make it happen.

Gotta go fast? Mark your calendar for Nov. 12

A Guide to <i><em>The Gilded Age </em></i>Families: the Scotts, van Rhijns, and Russells

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Making sense of the cousins, fiancées, and adopted sons that keep us fascinated as viewers.

The Gilded Age is ultimately a story about society and class, so it’s unsurprising that family ties and marriages are central to the plot. That also means that it can be difficult to sort out who’s who and how they’re all related. As the fourth season continues to gather steam, here’s a guide to all the players in the Scott, van Rhijn, and Russell families.

Peggy grew up in a prosperous Black family but has a difficult relationship with her father, a pharmacist who is unhappy with her decision to become a writer. Peggy was previously married and had a son, Thomas, whom she believed was stillborn. She later learns that her father lied to her and that Thomas is alive and living with an adoptive family. She meets Marian Brook when she is traveling home to her family and begins living with the van Rhijn family. Peggy becomes a reporter for the prestigious Black newspaper, the Globe, and writes a novel. She becomes engaged to Dr. Kirkland, a physician from a prominent family who treats her when she is seriously ill.

Arthur is the father of Peggy and the husband of Dorothy. He was born into slavery but became the successful owner of a pharmacy in Brooklyn. He has a difficult relationship with his daughter.

Peggy’s mother, Dorothy, is a pianist from Maryland who meets her husband in New York City when he saves her from men who were following her. Peggy is her only child.

When Peggy learns that Thomas, the son she believed had died, is alive and living in Philadelphia with the Spring family that adopted him, he is 3 years old. Shortly before Peggy finds out about him, Thomas dies from scarlet fever along with his adoptive mother.

Dr. Kirkland is a physician whom Peggy’s parents bring to treat her when she has a serious respiratory illness. He knew Peggy’s father when he was a child, and Arthur’s pharmacy career inspired him to become a doctor. He and Peggy date and then become engaged at the end of season 3.

Mrs. Kirkland, Dr. Kirkland’s mother, is from a wealthy Newport family. She expects her son to marry someone from a more pedigreed family than Peggy, and when she meets Mr. and Mrs. Scott, she treats them poorly, emphasizing her family’s status and Arthur’s former enslavement. She doesn’t approve of Peggy’s political activism.

Dr. Kirkland’s father is a pastor in Newport who grew up in a wealthy and well-known family.

Agnes is a widow and mother to Oscar. She was married to Arnold van Rhijn, an investment banker from a wealthy, early New York family. She is an aunt to Marian and lives with her sister Ada in a beautiful home on East 61st Street.

Ada is a devoted sister to Agnes, whom she lives with, and aunt to Marian and Oscar. When she meets Reverend Luke Forte, they marry quickly, and Ada begins living with him in the church rectory, which upsets the balance between her and Agnes. When her husband dies, Ada unexpectedly inherits a fortune, making her now wealthier than her sister.

Marian is the niece of Ada and Agnes, the daughter of their estranged brother. After her father dies, Marian finds out she’s been left no money, and she comes to live with her aunts, from whom she had been estranged. She becomes engaged to the widower Dashiell, who is Agnes’s nephew by marriage and Oscar’s cousin from his father’s side, but she later ends it. She has an on-and-off relationship with Larry Russell, George and Bertha Russell’s son.

An Episcopal reverend, Luke meets Ada after moving to New York from Boston and assuming a position at the Episcopal Church of Saint Thomas. He dies shortly after marrying Ada, leaving her the textile fortune he inherited from his mother.

Aurora, who is a major figure on the society scene, is a cousin of Oscar and the niece of Agnes. She becomes close with Marian through their family connections. Aurora is devastated when her husband, Charles, asks for a divorce.

Oscar is a banker and the calculating young son of Agnes, who is gay and closeted but eager to marry a wealthy woman. He proposes to Gladys Russell, but he loses interest when he believes her family might go broke. Oscar has a secret relationship with John Adams IV, a descendant of the political family. In season 4, he marries Enid, a widow with a fortune who shares his interest in social climbing.

Enid was once a maid for the Russell family, but she was let go after trying to seduce George. Enid reappears in season 2 as the wife of Joshua Winterton, an older man, and inherits his money and his place in society when he dies. When Enid gets to know Oscar, they become close and eventually marry, making her the daughter-in-law of Agnes.

A Cavalier King Charles spaniel, Pumpkin is the constant companion of Ada. Larry Russell rescues Pumpkin from a scary run-in with a horse.

Bertha Russell and her husband, George, move to New York City after his work in the railroad industry makes them very wealthy. Bertha is calculating and desperate to be admitted into high-level Manhattan society. She’s eager for her daughter Gladys to marry someone with a pedigreed background. She is also the mother of Larry.

Bertha’s husband is a cutthroat, often cruel, railroad magnate with a distaste for union workers but is deeply loving toward his children, Gladys and Larry. He moves out of his home after believing that his wife’s interference in her children’s love lives is inappropriate.

Bertha and George’s oldest child is an aspiring architect and Harvard grad with a more modern approach to hierarchy and social norms than his mother. He has a flirtation with Marian Brook.

Gladys is the overprotected daughter of Bertha and George. Her mother is focused on finding the right kind of husband for Gladys. Gladys is proposed to by Oscar van Rhijn, but her father doesn’t allow her to marry him because he questions Oscar’s intent. Gladys wants to be with Billy Cotton, a man she met at a party, but her mother believes she should be with the Duke of Buckingham, Hector Vere, a royal figure. Gladys marries Hector and lives with him in England. They have a child.

Everyone is atwitter when the fancy Duke of Buckingham visits New York. Though he has royal lineage, he has no money. He marries Gladys, knowing that marrying her will bring him significant wealth.

The Marquess of Sidmouth is the son of Gladys and Hector. He is their only child.

Sarah is Hector’s sister and lives with him and Gladys in their castle.

Bertha has a tenuous relationship with her sister Monica, whom we first meet when she attends Gladys’s wedding.

SolidUltra Secures €3 Million for Modular Power Transformers Built for AI Data Centers

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

SolidUltra has secured €3 million to develop modular solid-state transformers for a new generation of AI data centers. The French company is targeting a particularly consequential part of the infrastructure stack: converting medium-voltage grid electricity into the 800-volt direct current that future high-density computing facilities are preparing to distribute.

Founded in June 2026 and based in Massy, France, SolidUltra secured the funding within three months of incorporation, backed by its founding partner, French power-electronics group Watt & Well. The money will support engineering, product development, industrialization, and early customer engagement ahead of targeted commercial deployments in 2028.

The investment comes as rising compute density makes electrical design an increasingly important constraint on AI expansion. Adding accelerators requires more than securing chips: operators also need equipment that can deliver power reliably without consuming excessive space or losing too much energy along the way.

Conventional data center power systems commonly combine a low-frequency transformer with additional conversion equipment before power reaches computing hardware. Each stage has its own electrical losses, cooling requirements, and physical footprint. Those trade-offs become harder to manage as operators plan for megawatt-scale racks.

SolidUltra is developing a medium-voltage solid-state transformer, or SST, intended to replace that arrangement with an integrated grid-to-800VDC conversion system. Here, “transformer” refers to electrical power equipment. The product supplies AI infrastructure rather than performing AI inference itself.

The company’s website adds detail about the modular design: it describes 100-kilowatt cells connected in series on the medium-voltage input and in parallel on the DC output, using automotive-grade silicon-carbide components. It also describes redundant, field-replaceable cells. That architecture is intended to let operators build larger systems from repeated power blocks rather than treating every capacity increase as a separate equipment design.

In its funding announcement, SolidUltra outlines a scaling range from hundreds of kilowatts to 10 megawatts and beyond. For a growing AI facility, the practical attraction is the ability to expand power-conversion capacity alongside computing requirements.

SolidUltra is developing into a wider industry transition. NVIDIA’s 800VDC roadmap describes approaches for both existing AC-powered facilities and new, DC-native AI factories, with work through the Open Compute Project to align equipment requirements across the ecosystem.

The underlying electrical advantage is straightforward: at a given power level, a higher distribution voltage requires less current. Lower current can reduce conductor demands and resistive losses, making it easier to move substantial power through a constrained physical space. An 800VDC supply is still an intermediate distribution voltage; computing hardware needs further conversion before electricity reaches the chips.

There is also an important distinction between installing local conversion equipment in an existing facility and designing a new power chain around direct medium-voltage conversion. SolidUltra is pursuing the latter opportunity. Its alignment with the 800VDC transition does not, by itself, establish a commercial partnership with NVIDIA.

SolidUltra is targeting 98.5% conversion efficiency, power losses three times lower than conventional architectures, and an equipment footprint reduced by a factor of four. It also targets approximately 30% lower total cost of ownership and up to a tenfold reduction in the carbon footprint associated with power-conversion infrastructure.

These are development targets for the planned product, rather than independently demonstrated operating results from commercial installations. The carbon claim concerns the conversion infrastructure, while the cost and loss comparisons depend on the conventional system used as the baseline. Conversion efficiency should also be distinguished from the efficiency of an entire data center.

Modularity is part of the reliability plan as well. The company says its field-replaceable power modules are designed to be swapped in under 15 minutes, with an availability target of 99.999%. The objective is to make maintenance more granular, reducing the amount of capacity affected when an individual module needs attention.

SolidUltra also aims to shorten equipment deployment from years to weeks through a factory-built design. Faster installation of conversion hardware could help operators bring prepared sites online sooner, but it does not remove separate requirements for utility capacity, grid connections, permits, or the rest of a facility’s electrical system.

SolidUltra was co-founded by CEO Raul Iglesias, Chief Business Officer Romain Beaume, Alain Rouault, and Watt & Well. Its model combines a dedicated company focused on AI infrastructure with an established partner’s power-electronics engineering and manufacturing capabilities.

Watt & Well’s existing portfolio provides useful context. The company develops modular power equipment for electric-vehicle charging, including AC/DC and DC/DC applications, as well as bidirectional conversion products for battery energy storage. It also serves demanding energy and aerospace applications. That experience is relevant to designing and producing power hardware, although SolidUltra’s proposed data center system still has its own development and qualification work ahead.

“AI is not only changing computing. It is fundamentally changing the electrical infrastructure required to power it,” Iglesias said in the funding announcement. He described the investment as a way to move toward industrial deployment with Watt & Well’s backing.

Following its planned 2028 commercial launch, SolidUltra aims to scale production to 10,000 modular systems by 2032, representing approximately one gigawatt of installed power. That is a production ambition, not a disclosed order backlog.

The €3 million funding gives the company resources to advance the engineering and industrial work between its proposed architecture and deployable equipment. Its opportunity rests on a growing mismatch: AI racks are becoming denser, while the electrical infrastructure serving them takes considerable time and space to build. A compact, modular conversion system could address part of that mismatch—if SolidUltra can deliver its performance, maintenance, and manufacturing targets at commercial scale.

Deadpool 2's Rob Delaney Debunks Popular X-Men Fan Theory About His Character Peter

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

No, X-Men fans, Peter from "Deadpool 2" and "Deadpool & Wolverine" is not Peter Wisdom. Peter actor Rob Delaney confirmed this in a red carpet interview with ScreenRant, and we offer our deepest nerd condolences to anyone who may have been invested in this.

The presence of Delaney's character Peter, a supporting player in David Leitch's 2018 action/comedy "Deadpool 2," is a joke unto itself. Deadpool (Ryan Reynolds) is assembling a team of superheroes called the X-Force that he intends to employ for a skydiving-based rescue mission. Deadpool is such a non-credible deadbeat, though, that he can't afford to be too choosy about who joins his team. He recruits characters like Shatterstar (Lewis Tan), Domino (Zazie Beetz), and Bedlam (Terry Crews), but also deigns to take on Peter, a regular, kind of dorky middle-aged guy with no superpowers.

When the trailer for "Deadpool 2" first dropped, /Film theorized that Delaney could be playing Peter Wisdom, an X-Men character with deep ties to X-Force. The character was first introduced in an issue of the Britain-set X-Men comic "Excalibur," dated February of 1995, and, like all comic book characters, he underwent multiple dramatic life events in a very short amount of time. He dated Shadowcat, became the mentor for X-Force, seemingly died, returned, and became the head of a spy agency. In the comics, Pete Wisdom can absorb heat from the environment around him and channel it into magical "hot knives" that protrude from his fingers.

The character also starred in his own series of comics in 2006 called simply "Wisdom," and he likely has at least a few fans out there. For those who think he appeared in the "Deadpool" movies, though, that wasn't Peter Wisdom. That was just Peter.

The issue of Peter's identity may be a little confusing, as sources like Wikipedia list Rob Delaney as definitely playing Pete Wisdom in both "Deadpool 2" and the 2024 follow-up "Deadpool & Wolverine." On IMDb, however, his proper credit of just "Peter" is retained. In the red carpet interview, Delaney was 100% unambiguous on the matter, saying:

"Peter Wisdom is a guy from the comics. My character is not Peter Wisdom. He's just a guy named Peter. And I think maybe they arbitrarily gave him the last initial 'W,' which explains the confusion. But in fact, [he's] just Peter, created from whole cloth by the writers."

Sometimes actors in Marvel Comics-based movies have to be coy about spoilers and plot details, but it seems that Delaney is not being coy in this case. He seems to genuinely be saying he isn't playing Pete Wisdom.

The arc of Delaney's actual character is pretty hilarious unto itself. Peter is recruited into X-Force, meets a grisly fate, is saved through the miracle of time travel, and then proves to be deeply beloved across the multiverse in "Deadpool & Wolverine." Peter turns up in that film's climax, which involves hundreds of parallel-universe Deadpools pouring through an interdimensional portal to fight each other. When Peter arrives on the scene, however, every Deadpool pauses to marvel at his presence. It seems that all Deadpools have a Peter in their home dimension, and that they all hold him in very high esteem. The hundreds of Deadpools embrace Peter, and he enjoys the attention.

With a universe as long-lived and complex as Marvel Comics, of course, some fans may happily chime in with an optimistic "never say die" attitude about Rob Delaney playing Pete Wisdom. Indeed, as "Deadpool & Wolverine" proved, every character in the Marvel Cinematic Universe has an untold number of parallel doppelgängers from all across the multiverse. Even if the original Pete Wisdom is a suave British James Bond-type with heat knife powers, and the movie Peter is a dippy American everyman, there's no reason why those characters can't be one and the same across the multiverse, right?

And, heck, "Deadpool 2" made a lot of vital changes to known comic book characters, including the aforementioned Shatterstar, a.k.a Rusty. In the comics, Shatterstar is an interdimensional traveler from Mojoworld, something he does explain in "Deadpool 2." But in the movie, he doesn't become a vital member of the team. Indeed, he has an unfortunate run-in with a helicopter. Deadpool, thereafter, quips that no one is going to miss Shatterstar. Nonetheless, the character returned for a very brief cameo in "Deadpool & Wolverine."

So why not do something like that for Pete Wisdom? As of this writing, /Film has been gathering information about the upcoming X-Men film from Marvel Studios, citing a familiar line-up and keeping track of pieces of casting. (Inde Navarrette from "Obsession" has been cast as the character of Rogue, for instance.) Time will tell if the makers of the new X-Men will have enough of a sense of humor to cast Rob Delaney as Pete Wisdom.
