Vanoli: ‘Fiorentina ought to feel angry at 2-2 Juventus draw’

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Paolo Vanoli insists Fiorentina ‘ought to feel angry’ they only drew 2-2 with Juventus after leading twice, but this team ‘is on the right path and it was a strong signal.’

The Viola had started to dream of beating their bitter rivals at the Stadio Artemio Franchi, especially after taking the lead in the opening four minutes with a Mateo Pellegrino header.

However, Nick Woltemade drew level, and when Alieu Njie bundled in at the back post, that too was cancelled out by the Gleison Bremer header on a corner.

Radu Dragusin had the ball in the net for a third time, but it was disallowed for the defender’s push on Bremer.

“I think we had a great performance against a strong opponent,” Vanoli told DAZN Italia.

“We honoured what our fans asked for, and it must disappoint us that we did not get the full three points. These are young players and we ought to give them more confidence, I think we played well today, and also know there is a lot of work to be done.”

There were some surprising choices in the starting XI, bringing in Pedro Goncalves and Christ Inao Oulai rather than Marco Brescianini and Niccolò Fagioli.

“Before kick-off, people said my line-up was courageous. If we want to play football, then it’s not about using players who are more defensive or more offensive, it’s about everyone working hard and chasing down opponents,” added Vanoli.

“I think today these lads did that. I feel this is the right path, we need to do better and have more determination on the ball, or when winning it back, which is something we still don’t do well enough. These are things we need to improve, but it was still a strong signal.

“My players ought to be angry, because even if they are young and inexperienced, they should be upset at putting in such a great performance and not winning the game. We all expect more.”

Pellegrino was on target today, but has seemed at times a little isolated in attack when Fiorentina play.

“We are trying to give him better service, but above all get closer to him, as we tend to give him the ball and then aren’t ready for when he holds it up. Franco Mastantuono also needs to come between the lines and dialogue with Pellegrino, which Pedro Goncalves does better because he is more accustomed to that cut inside.

“This is a team that wants to do something important, we are on the right track and must keep working on it,” confessed Vanoli.

Fiorentina have at least managed to scramble their way out of the relegation zone with a three-round unbeaten run, but are only two points clear of Monza.

This also extends their positive streak against Juventus, managing two wins and three stalemates since the 1-0 defeat in April 2024.

Soul Glo’s Diaspora Problems is a hardcore record like no other

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Diaspora Problems is one of the 21st century’s best punk albums.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Diaspora Problems is one of the 21st century’s best punk albums.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Soul Glo has a new record called NA I’M GON GRAB ALLAT coming out in January, and I, for one, can not wait. While two singles have dropped, it’s not enough to truly tide me over, so I’ve been revisiting Songs to Yeet at the Sun and my favorite album of 2022 — Diaspora Problems.

“Gold Chain Punk (whogonbeatmyass?)” is an opening salvo for the ages. Bong rip, into a catchy-as-hell hardcore riff, with frontman Pierce Jordan ranting. Then comes the half-time stomp as Jordan gets fully unhinged, screaming “Who gon beat my ass? Who the fuck gon beat my ass?” From here on out, as the music shifts through a few different sections, the energy never comes down. Jordan shreds his vocal cords, begging, “Can I live?” Before finally spelling it out for anyone still confused:

Say more, say less, it’s all the sameWhen it’s time to die we can meet outsideTo see who gon feel my pain

The record barely lets up from here on out. “Coming Correct” opens with a cough and a steady 808, giving you literally three seconds to catch your breath before Jordan jumps on the mic shouting, “I try to listen the way, I wanna be listened to.” The song starts stacking elements quickly, reaching full firestorm by 25 seconds in, and then doesn’t let up for the rest of its three-minute runtime.

The front chunk of the record is just perfect unrelenting hardcore song after perfect unrelenting hardcore song. Jordan’s crazed charisma is undeniable as he ricochets between leftist rhetoric, goofy bits about smoking weed, and vulnerable explorations of his struggles with depression. You’d be forgiven for struggling to keep up with his rapid-fire delivery, which owes almost as much to modern rappers as it does to classic hardcore acts like Minor Threat or Bad Brains.

Track six, “Driponomics,” fully embraces those more modern influences with trap snares, wall-rattling 808 kicks, and a guest verse from Mother Maryrose. The sampled guitar calls to mind the sax squeals favored by the Bomb Squad… just heavier.

The album eventually culminates in “Spiritual Level Of Gang Shit,” which features guest verses from McKinley Dixon and Lojii over gently echoed guitar melodies and dub-inspired groove. Then, around the 2:20 mark, things take a turn as the guitars turn punishing and the drums shift to a standard hardcore beat. But the song hasn’t reached its final form yet. That comes almost a minute later when the horn section kicks in.

Next to other modern hardcore standard bearers like Turnstile, Soul Glo might almost seem like traditionalists heavily influenced by the DC scene. But Diaspora Problems shows their ambition and willingness to take risks. Soul Glo plays with genre, is confrontational in its politics, and is generally just uncompromising.

Oh, and they put on a hell of a live show (I’ve seen them at least four times now). Don’t miss their incredible Tiny Desk Concert either, which I’m pretty sure is the first mosh pit to even open up in the NPR offices.

Soul Glo’s Diaspora Problems is available on Bandcamp and most major streaming services, including Qobuz, Deezer, YouTube Music, Apple Music, and Spotify.

The Jackery HomePower 2000 Plus v2 Red Cross Edition — CleanTechnica Tested

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Jackery was one of the earliest portable power station producers to bring these beautiful creations into the mainstream. They have a line of portable power stations and proprietary portable, foldable solar panels that continues to make them one of the top go-to options for anyone looking for power on the go.

Recently, they launched a Red Cross Edition of their popular HomePower 2000 Plus v2 that donates a portion of the profits to the Red Cross to support first responders. We covered these limited edition units when they first hit our radar during our visit to Jackery’s headquarters in Shenzhen, China, and now they are finally moving into production.

Jackery donates 10% of profits from this series to donate portable power stations to rescue organizations and emergency response teams, supporting disaster relief and recovery efforts. At the volumes Jackery is producing, that’s a meaningful step in the right direction to putting portable power stations into the hands of the first responders who need them most.

Disclaimer: Jackery provided the Red Cross edition of the 2000 Pro V2 portable power station to the author for the purposes of this review.

Charging up the unit is a simple matter thanks to its ability to pull a charge from multiple sources. Right out of the gate, you can charge from a normal 120V AC wall outlet at around 1,600 watts. This is typically the maximum you can safely charge from any wall outlet without overheating cables or outlets, and the Jackery HomePower 2000 Plus V2 makes full use of it. In our testing, we pulled 1,590W from the wall for a full charge from empty in an hour and a half.

You can also charge up the unit with solar panels, which with 800 watts of solar can recharge the battery from fully drained to overflowing in 3.2 hours. Jackery uses a proprietary solar connector, so keep that in mind before you buy your battery and/or panels.

You can also charge it up via your car’s 12V automotive outlet (requires optional car charging adapter for $29.90), which can push power out at about 100 watts. This varies slightly from car to car, but the fact of the matter is that you’re generally limited based on the factory wiring for what that outlet can handle.

Jackery knew about that, so they developed their own DC-to-DC charger for combustion vehicles. This optional accessory costs $239, but taps into the full power of your alternator and can pull up to 600 watts. This is a faster charge and can top up the battery in about 4 hours, but requires a bit of extra planning and work and assumes you’re running a combustion vehicle. It’s worth consulting with a professional if you’re going to go this route, as alternator wiring and max output varies greatly from car to car.

Electric vehicles typically don’t have a 12V outlet, opting for USB-C instead. Unfortunately, the Jackery HomePower 2000 Plus V2 Red Cross Edition is not able to charge up via USB-C at any power level. Fortunately, many electric cars come with integrated AC outlets, which can be used to charge the battery up in a pinch.

I tested this in my Cybertruck and achieved similar charging rates to what I was able to achieve at home, which tops the battery up in about an hour and a half. This is a great option to use while fast charging on a road trip because you can charge for about 30 minutes at a time to keep your portable power station charged up without having to run the inverter while driving, which drains the battery faster.

When it’s time to tap into all that stored power, the Jackery HomePower 2000 Plus v2 Red Cross Edition is ready. It can push out 2,400 watts of continuous power with surge capability of up to 4,800W. Given that this is a compact 2 kWh unit weighing just 21 lb, that’s a pretty solid level of power output that it can handle. Power output has continuously been improving with each successive generation and Jackery continues to be near the top of the pack with its numbers.

It’s obviously up to the task of powering lightweight items like drones, laptops, portable lights, charging radios, keeping the internet on, and even powering your fridge in an emergency. On the home front, it’s also a great option. Thanks to its LFP chemistry, Jackery expects you to get 6,000 charging cycles out of this battery, which equates to more than 16 years of daily cycling.

Even though it is such a compact unit, it should be able to keep essentials running at home — including your refrigerator, freezer, computer, lights, smartphone, Wi-Fi, etc. — for nearly 10 hours. That should get you through most outages, and, honestly, if you’re having longer outages than that, you probably want to look into a home battery system. Or you can tap into the HomePower 2000 Plus v2 Red Cross Edition’s ace up its sleeve….

The Jackery HomePower 2000 Plus V2 Red Cross edition can actually add more capacity. It has a special port on the left side of the unit that lets you plug in any of Jackery’s Battery Plus expansion batteries for up to 12kWh of total capacity. That’s a 6× increase over this unit’s installed capacity and lets you scale your storage capacity as you find new use cases for it.

The use case for this at home is really keeping the lights and and the refrigerator running during an emergency and using solar or a generator to charge up when it runs low. It can absorb up to 800 watts of solar from an attached array, meaning you can fully charge it in about an hour and a half depending on the sunlight you’re getting. Even in terrible conditions, it’s still going to charge up in just a couple of hours.

We put this beast to use running a portable refrigerator, lights, USB, charging our laptop and phones, and even charging a few portable batteries for us to take with us hiking. This unit kept our devices charged up for several days of fun in the desert. It was great knowing that after we were done charging our devices, the battery would top itself up again from the attached solar panels.

No matter how many devices we plugged in, including our usual kitchen torture test, its 2,400W of power output was more than enough to keep everything running. The surge power is pretty cool too because it allows the unit to absorb the spikes in consumption that come from a lot of devices when you first turn them on.

But we wanted more. So we pulled out some new winter heaters and really made the Jackery sweat. Each of these heating units can pull up to 1,500 watts each, which is about the max you can get from a standard 15A outlet. We plugged both into the Jackery and cranked them up to the max. I’m personally thrilled to have more energy-hungry appliances to torture — I mean test — portable power stations with.

Playing with all of their settings, we had them pulling almost 3,100 watts constantly before the Jackery finally decided to throw in the towel. It kept them running for several minutes over its own 2,400 watt constant power output rating, which was impressive, especially considering how compact this thing is.

It’s exciting to see Jackery continuing to push into new battery technologies, but in this case, this is the same battery they sell as a normal unit, just rebranded to highlight the great work being done at the Red Cross and to give some of the profits back in the form of product.

That’s worth celebrating, and it’s exciting to see Jackery leading the charge to enable first responders with technology without charging governments or consumers more money for the same product.

Or maybe you just like a bright red unit that’ll make it hard to lose or forget. Whether you’re heading out on a camping trip or heading back to the house after a long week out in nature, the bright red colorway of this unit is sure to stand out.

Kakao Mobility Signs NVIDIA MOU for Level 4 Autonomous Driving

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Kakao Mobility announced on November 9, 2026, that it had signed a memorandum of understanding with NVIDIA to develop Level 4 autonomous-driving technology, initially combining their systems on Kia purpose-built vehicles (PBVs).

The planned work pairs Kakao Mobility’s autonomous-driving AI and mobility platform with NVIDIA’s computing, sensing and safety technology. The stated goal is a Level 4 system aligned with global standards, building on urban driving that Kakao Mobility says has validated its technology in Seoul’s Gangnam district.

Kakao Mobility is progressively developing an end-to-end autonomous-driving model that connects perception through decision-making in one model. It plans to introduce an autonomous-driving computing unit containing two NVIDIA DRIVE AGX Thor chips, intended to process large-scale AI computation in real time. That hardware plan connects the model’s development directly to the compute capacity required to run it in a vehicle.

NVIDIA describes Hyperion as a standardized reference architecture integrating compute, sensors, networking and safety systems, with DRIVE AGX Thor forming part of the platform. Kakao Mobility intends to use the architecture to optimize connections among the vehicle, sensors and computing unit while pursuing system stability and compatibility.

The safety work extends into software development and validation through NVIDIA Halos. NVIDIA describes Halos OS as a three-layer safety architecture built on ASIL D-certified DriveOS foundations, integrating safety middleware and deployable safety applications. Together, Hyperion and Halos supply the system architecture and safety framework around Kakao Mobility’s own driving software.

The companies will prioritize integrated development on Kia PBVs under Kakao Mobility’s July 2026 partnership with Kia to develop PBVs for autonomous-driving services. They identify the Kia PV5 as the initial model for the combined technology. The plan is to develop and supply vehicles for Level 4 services using Kakao Mobility’s software alongside NVIDIA’s AI computing and safety systems.

That gives the agreement a specific integration path: Kakao Mobility contributes the driving software and mobility platform, NVIDIA is to contribute computing and safety technology, and the Kia PBV is the first vehicle target. Commercialization remains an objective of that development work.

Kakao Mobility CEO Ryu Gung-seon said the collaboration is intended to accelerate end-to-end development and prepare for Level 4 commercialization through an ecosystem spanning software, components and finished vehicles. NVIDIA automotive vice president Rishi Dhall framed the nearer objective as accelerating safe and scalable autonomous-driving services in South Korea.

The companies are also leaving open medium- to long-term global expansion, based on technology validated in South Korea. The immediate test is integration on the PV5: turning the separate software, compute, safety and vehicle contributions into a validated Level 4 system is the next step toward the commercial driverless service described in the announcement.

It affects your breathing a little bit, so [I] wasn’t feeling well, couldn’t go on with the show, took an EpiPen shot, got treated by EMTs, felt better about 8, 9 o’clock came along, but it takes a long while to get out of your system,” Tirico said during an appearance on Today in 2025. “And thank goodness Ahmed Fareed, who’s great, was there and filled in more than abley. The show went on without us.”

From the Archives: When Iowa Governor-Elect Rob Sand Was Shot by Steven Meisel for Vogue Italia

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

“Be My Baby,” by Steven Meisel, was originally published in the February 2004 issue of Vogue Italia.

For more of the best from Vogue’s archive, sign up for our Nostalgia newsletter here.

With a fluffed-up quiff and a supermodel on his arm, here’s a look at Iowa’s next governor. Long before Democrat Rob Sand made political history, he was having a very different kind of moment in the spotlight, posing for Steven Meisel in the pages of Vogue Italia.

The year was 2004, and Sand was among the fresh faces who appeared in “Be My Baby,” an editorial in the February issue, photographed by Meisel and styled by Brana Wolf. Alongside Sand was a cast of rising fashion stars, including a young Caroline Trentini, with whom he posed in a series of moody, black-and-white portraits.

The concept centered on a romanticized vision of 1950s youth culture, abounding with the aforementioned rockabilly hair and lots of leather The original Italian introduction described the mood as a collision of bon ton—good manners and traditional propriety—and transgression.

Garren was responsible for the towering and teased hairstyles, while Dame Pat McGrath crafted smudgy, kohl-rimmed eyes. The cast wore Belstaff leather biker jackets, white Guess T-shirts and jeans, Helmut Lang tank tops and tailoring, Prada separates, and a Gucci crocodile belt. Womenswear included diaphanous Blumarine slips, Prada silk and tweed, and chiffon dresses by Rossella Tarabini for Anna Molinari.

“Was a fun day!” Sand wrote beneath an Instagram post by Meisel revisiting the shoot.

Sand’s modeling career was no mere college dalliance. While studying political science at Brown University, the six-foot-three Iowan spent time working in Milan and Paris, walking for Prada, cult Japanese label Number (N)ine, and Giuliano Fujiwara. He also appeared in Prada’s spring 2003 menswear show. In fact, his fashion commitments were once so serious that he missed the 2004 Iowa caucuses in his hometown of Decorah because he was working in Milan.

On November 3, Sand defeated Republican Zach Lahn to become the first Democrat elected governor of Iowa in 20 years, following two terms as state auditor. His newly resurfaced modeling portfolio has since become an unlikely internet sensation. Sand himself has joined in on the joke, sharing one of his old Vogue Italia photographs with the caption: “I’m disappointed to learn images of me modeling for Prada are circulating. Because this shot from Italian Vogue is much better.”

From Prada to public office, it’s quite the career trajectory. And with his campaign now selling “My Model Governor” merchandise, Rob Sand clearly knows how to harness a good fashion moment.

Kyle Larson Secures His Third NASCAR Cup Championship in Opening Stage of Championship Race

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

With his fourth-place finish in the first stage of the championship race at Homestead-Miami, Kyle Larson is a three-time NASCAR Cup Series Champion.

Chase Briscoe blew up with three laps remaining in the opening stage of the NASCAR Cup Series Season finale at Homestead-Miami, ending the stage early while Kyle Larson ran fourth.

With NASCAR’s new rule to end the stage if the caution is within 10 laps of the end, Larson took home the minimum seven points needed to clinch the Championship.

Larson was told he was one point shy over the radio, but he was 64 points above Christopher Bell and 76 points above Bell. He clinched the championship.

Kyle Larson joins the likes of Lee Petty, David Pearson, Cale Yarborough, Darrel Waltrip, Tony Stewart, and Joey Logano as a three-time NASCAR Cup Series Champion.

Through The Chase, Larson had an average of 49.5 points per race, easily rising to the top of the standings and then pacing the field from there.

Entering Homestead, it wasn’t given, but it was likely he could clinch the championship in stage one.

Spalletti: ‘Juventus wallow in doubt and lose confidence’

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Luciano Spalletti reiterated Juventus ‘can’t seem to be consistent’ and ‘lose confidence, so we wallow in doubt’ after a 2-2 draw with Fiorentina, but remained enigmatic on penalty appeals.

The Bianconeri had to fight back twice for a point in Florence this evening, as they went behind within four minutes to a Mateo Pellegrino header, then once Nick Woltemade had equalised with his first Serie A goal, Alieu Njie had restored the Viola advantage.

Gleison Bremer rose to meet a Douglas Luiz corner kick for the final 2-2 result, although both teams had late complaints against the referee.

“We tend to drown in a glass of water sometimes, and lose confidence in the way we play,” Spalletti told DAZN Italia.

“We conceded so early, but then reacted well, we just can’t seem to be consistent throughout a match, as a team or as individuals. We do really well for 10 minutes, then are lacking in something else the next, we aren’t consistent.”

The Njie goal raised eyebrows, because while Guglielmo Vicario had made the initial save, his teammates weren’t reactive enough to clear, allowing the Fiorentina player to bundle in the loose ball.

“In some moments we decided not to make decisions, we just stay there and accept the situation, without taking action or the initiative. Doing something takes away the doubt, but when you wallow in the doubt, you never manage to be in control of a situation,” confessed Spalletti.

It was a new trident attack this evening with Jeremie Boga and Randal Kolo Muani taking the wings, leaving Woltemade as the centre-forward.

“We made this choice because Fiorentina are aggressive, and seeing as we don’t build out too well from the back, at times we need to be more direct going for the centre-forward. It is not his greatest strength, holding up the ball, as he likes to combine with his teammates, but he’s the best at it in the squad.

“Kolo Muani is doing better, and with these fitness levels and rediscovered verve, you can put him anywhere and he’ll deliver a performance. He moves around a lot more and seems more engaged with the rest of the team.”

Radu Dragusin’s goal was disallowed for a push on Bremer, while Juve had penalty appeals rejected for a potential Joao Mario handling offence.

“I don’t know, do you have an alternative question?” replied Spalletti with a smile when shown the replay of the incident.

“I didn’t need a replay, I already saw it on the pitch.”

The result means that Juventus go into the break for international duty in seventh place, falling six adrift of Milan and the Champions League qualifying spots.

A tally of 18 points after 11 rounds is their worst start to a Serie A season since 15 points in 2021/22 under Max Allegri.

These 7 Burger Chains Use Frozen Ground Beef — Is Your Favorite On The List?

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Fast food chains have undergone an interesting transformation in recent years. They're under increasing pressure from customers and regulators alike to offer more nutritious, less-processed food, and much of the debate centers on whether they use fresh or frozen beef in their kitchens. Certain chains, like Chipotle, have even made their commitment to fresh ingredients a central part of their brand identity. But while fresh meat is certainly having a moment, frozen burger patties aren't going anywhere any time soon.

Frozen beef patties play a foundational role in the fast food industry. To operate on the scale of giants like McDonald's, which sells millions of burgers a day, it goes without saying that you're going to need a serious amount of beef. That's where frozen has a serious edge. Fresh, raw beef has a relatively short shelf life, whereas frozen beef can be stored for months. That makes frozen patties a practical way to keep up with demand and avoid having large quantities of meat spoil before they can be used. They also offer consistency. Because patties can be made to precise specifications and frozen until needed, they're uniform in size and weight, helping ensure that every burger cooks at roughly the same rate and comes out looking like the last.

One of the biggest assumptions surrounding fresh and frozen beef is that fresh automatically means better. But this isn't really fair. Freezing beef doesn't do anything to its nutritional profile, and patties are often frozen soon after production to preserve their freshness. Better yet, some chefs even swear by cooking burgers from frozen for a juicier texture and perfectly browned crust. Bet you didn't see that coming. If you still don't believe us, odds are that one of your favorite fast food burgers is living proof of this.

As the largest fast food chain in the U.S., it probably won't come as much of a surprise that McDonald's relies heavily on frozen beef for its burgers, as well as breakfast items like its McMuffins, McGriddles, and bagels. Its frozen beef patties are cooked for around 40 seconds between two heated metal plates before being transferred to heated holding cabinets — that's right, McDonald's patties aren't made-to-order. For decades, this was standard practice across the menu, but in March 2018, the chain shook things up by officially rolling out fresh, never-frozen beef for its Quarter Pounder line.

Burger King trails McDonald's as the second-largest burger chain in the U.S., but one area where it hasn't followed its rival's lead is fresh beef. While McDonald's offers one fresh option, Burger King still uses frozen beef across its entire burger menu. The patties are shaped and flash-frozen at centralized processing facilities before being shipped to individual restaurants. And despite the chain's famous claim that its burgers are "flame-grilled," that description doesn't tell the whole story. Rather than being grilled individually over an open flame, the frozen patties travel along a conveyor belt through a flame-broiling oven, where they're exposed to gas flames as they cook. It's this process that gives Burger King burgers their distinctive charred exterior and smoky flavor.

Famous for early adoption of the fast-food drive-thru intercom system (which In-N-Out pioneered), Jack in the Box is best known for its delightfully eclectic menu, which hosts everything from tacos and curly fries to, of course, burgers. There are plenty of unique options to choose from, including the Sourdough Jack, served on toasted sourdough bread, and its newer line of trendy smash-style burgers. But one thing they all have in common is that their patties arrive at the kitchen frozen. So don't expect to find any fresh beef in Jack in the Box. The chain sticks to old-school griddling on a flat-top, but at the same time, it has piloted a batch of futuristic burger-flipping robots at certain locations in San Diego.

A trip to Dairy Queen is never a bad idea when you're craving something sweet and frozen, but did you know that you can also order a burger to go with your Blizzard? Despite revealing that its burgers don't contain human meat (yes, this really happened), the chain is yet to share whether its beef patties arrive fresh or frozen. Given that we're talking about Dairy Queen here, you'd be forgiven for assuming they spend some time chilling in a freezer. And according to one self-identified employee, that's exactly the case. "[The patties] come frozen," they revealed in a Reddit comment. "We have a 'grill' where we put the patties on a conveyor belt that goes over a flame. Then, they drop down from the machine into baskets, where we put them in a warmer with other patties."

White Castle holds an iconic place in American fast food history as one of the country's oldest burger chains. Founded in 1921, the chain originally used fresh beef, but it didn't take long to make the switch to more convenient frozen patties in 1931. Today, White Castle might not be on the same level as giants like McDonald's and Burger King, but we'll give it points for originality. Its cooking method is truly unlike anything you'll find at most other burger chains. First, a bed of onions is spread across a hot grill, followed by the frozen beef patties, with the buns placed on top. Rather than being seared directly against the cooking surface, the patties are steamed by the moisture rising from the onions. The small holes punched into each patty allow the steam to pass through, cooking the beef from below while warming the buns above.

Everything about Sonic is gloriously retro, but there's nothing old-school about how the chain prepares its burgers. Before your burger is (literally) rolled out to your car, workers pull the patties straight from the freezer and place them on a flat-top grill, flipping them as they cook. Like many frozen fast-food patties, Sonic's burgers are also perforated with small holes. To those who have never worked in a fast-food kitchen before, these little bumps and ridges might look alarming at first, but there's really nothing to worry about. They're simply there to help the frozen patties cook more quickly and evenly.

Carl's Jr. is a SoCal institution and has been serving up burgers since 1941. Like Burger King, the frozen patties are passed through a gas-flame conveyor belt, known as charbroiling. Though fans might be disappointed to learn that the chain's (rather pricey) burgers start out frozen, Carl Jr. is actively looking to level up its quality by promising that each and every burger will eventually be cooked-to-order. This means that customers will receive a hotter, fresher burger rather than a batch-cooked one that's been left sitting forgotten in a heating cabinet. As of writing, Carl's has released no official statement on whether the so-called "Burger Revolution" also includes non-frozen patties.

Survival after sudden cardiac arrest didn’t improve despite training bystanders, emergency responders

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Test your knowledge with our new weekday mini crossword

Liz covers cardiovascular and metabolic disease, asking why we’re not doing better or even losing ground against the world’s leading killer. Send tips on Signal at LizC.22.

CHICAGO — Sudden cardiac arrest statistics are chilling. In the United States, 350,000 people’s heartbeats, breathing, pulse, and blood circulation will all halt outside hospitals this year, seemingly out of the blue. These are not heart attacks. Only 1 in 10 people will survive them.

CPR and defibrillation can save their lives (and brains), but that depends on bystanders and emergency response systems being both nearby and prepared to step in — immediately. Survival chances drop by 10% for every minute that goes by without CPR.

Hints from observational studies suggested better, more comprehensive community training can help, which means educating 911 dispatchers, first responders, and potential bystanders. But results from a randomized controlled trial presented Sunday at the American Heart Association’s scientific sessions and published in the New England Journal of Medicine on Sunday show that approach has not worked, or at least not yet. But examples in Denmark may point the way forward.

“We performed an efficient, large, and high-quality trial engaging large segments of the community in emergency response, and in spite of this, survival remained low and unchanged,” Carolina Malta Hansen, the study’s lead author, said at a media briefing. “A multifaceted community and EMS implementation strategy did not improve survival with a favorable neurologic outcome for patients with out-of-hospital cardiac arrest. However, in the final year, there appeared to be an improvement in bystanders.”

Starting in 2021, researchers randomly assigned participants in 60 counties in North Carolina to receive intensive education on cardiac arrest intervention or to continue with standard practice. The intervention entailed training 911 dispatchers to improve their rapid recognition of cardiac arrest and better guide bystanders through CPR. First responders were educated on the use of automated external defibrillators before emergency services arrived. Community members in workplaces, religious groups, government offices, and other settings received training in CPR.

About 8.5 million people live in these counties. During the study’s five years, 22,459 adult residents experienced cardiac arrest outside the hospital. Their median age was 64; 37% were women, 33% were Black; and 29% lived in rural areas.

The numbers were split this way: 11,615 people experienced out-of-hospital cardiac arrest in counties that received intensive education, and 10,844 people had out-of-hospital cardiac arrest in communities that did not receive the intervention. The education strategy did not result in more patients surviving than patients with usual care.

Amrish Deshmukh, a cardiac electrophysiologist who treats patients with heart rhythm disorders at the University of Michigan Health Frankel Cardiovascular Center, called the study results disappointing, but not surprising.

“Many interventions that have been studied in out-of-hospital cardiac arrest and in post-arrest care unfortunately have not been shown to improve survival or neurologic outcomes,” he told STAT in an email. He was not involved in the new trial. “Prior studies have shown that mobile phone alerts, volunteer training, and increased automated external defibrillator (AEDs) availability can increase the performance of CPR and use of AEDs, but not all have shown improvements in survival.”

And yet, study author Malta Hansen, a cardiologist and clinical research associate professor at Herlev and Gentofte Hospital and Copenhagen EMS in Denmark, is hopeful. In the trial’s fourth year, an exploratory analysis showed the percentage of patients who underwent CPR performed by a bystander increased, suggesting that this component of the intervention was effective. More time may be needed to see which elements mattered and whether they affect survival.

“In light of the apparent improvement in CPR for the last year, further evaluation in the longer, more intense intervention is important,” she said.

Malta Hansen cites the case of Denmark, where rates of bystander CPR and survival are much higher than in the United States. The North Carolina study trained almost 5% of the population, at which point researchers started seeing a difference in delivery of CPR between the group that received training and the group that did not.

“Obviously, that’s not enough. We have observational evidence from other countries like Denmark, where 80% of the population is trained and 75% of patients receive high-speed care,” she said.

In Denmark, the defibrillation rate is 11%, compared to 2% in the U.S. In Denmark, survival is about 16%, compared to 10% in the U.S.

That CPR-trained rate in Denmark climbed from 20% in the early 2000s to 80% now, Malta Hansen said. “It’s not something that’s done in two or three years.”

Here’s what helps: To get your driver’s license in Denmark, you have to show that you have certification in CPR. “It’s just an incredibly efficient way to reach everybody,” she said.

Brahmajee Nallamothu, an interventional cardiologist and editor in chief of the AHA journal Circulation: Cardiovascular Quality and Outcomes, had another theory on timing. Starting the trial right as Covid-19 was emerging could have affected educational efforts and could explain why effects of the training were delayed. He also asked if the educational intervention was the right approach.

“I don’t think anyone would argue with the importance of early treatment in out-of-hospital cardiac arrest. When we think about bystander CPR, early defibrillation with AEDs, the challenge is: How do you translate that?” Nallamathu, who was not involved in the trial, said. “I think we still struggle with what’s the best way to train individuals so that they have sustained ability to understand and then utilize that tool when that situation arises, that’s an area that’s still complicated and still unknown.”

Malta Hansen’s team will follow this population in North Carolina for at least another year.

“We have established a fantastic infrastructure and network and collaboration, and everybody’s very excited to keep working on this,” she said. “What we need are communities who are very engaged and very eager to keep working on this. So we believe that future efforts to guide us will be successful.”

That won’t be easy, Michigan’s Deshmukh said.

“Implementation science is challenging (as evidenced by this and other negative trials), but is what I suspect will move the needle the most,” he wrote.

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Frenchman under pressure after four successive defeats

Former Spurs manager Frank emerging as main candidate

Crystal Palace are poised to sack Pierre Sage after Sunday’s defeat by Liverpool left them bottom of the Premier League for the first time since December 2017, with Thomas Frank emerging as the main candidate to succeed him.

The Frenchman, appointed in mid-June to replace Oliver Glasner, has been under mounting pressure since a 5-0 defeat by Brighton last month. There have been few signs of improvement as Palace have slumped to four successive league defeats.

Sage has guided Palace to only two wins from their 10 Premier League games this season and they have the league’s worst defensive record, with 24 goals conceded so far. Although there were promising moments during the 3-1 defeat by Liverpool at Selhurst Park on Sunday, their habit of conceding goals from set pieces has not been rectified and it is understood the patience of the club’s hierarchy has worn thin despite Sage only being at the helm for 16 matches.

He is expected to become the first Premier League manager to be sacked this season on Monday. Palace are already believed to have started the process of bringing in his replacement. Frank, who was dismissed by Tottenham in February, spoke to Palace in the summer and is said to be keen for a new challenge after a successful spell at Brentford.

The former Nottingham Forest manager Sean Dyche also has his backers at Palace and would be seen as a safe pair of hands given his vast experience in the Premier League. Vítor Pereira, another former Forest manager, could be an alternative.

Sage signed a three-year contract in south London after leaving Lens, where he won the Coupe de France and qualified for the Champions League in his final season. Palace have lost their two main central defenders this year, Marc Guéhi moving to Manchester City in January and Maxence Lacroix to Chelsea in the summer. Two loan players – Axel Disasi and the teenager Honest Ahanor – were signed as replacements. Palace did not land one of Sage’s top targets, Malick Fofana.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

Sage denied after the Liverpool game that his dream job had turned into a “nightmare” but suggested that Palace’s squad needed investment in January. “We have to adjust some positions during the winter transfer window to give more solutions,” he said.

Nvidia Launches Investigation Into CEO Jensen Huang Following Honeypot Scandal

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

What first emerged as tabloid gossip seems to be escalating into a dangerous scandal for graphics card titans Nvidia. In recent weeks, stories emerged that CEO Jensen Huang fell victim to a honeypot operation. One that could have contributed to $2 million worth of contraband breaking sanctions on China. Now the company says they will be launching a formal investigation into Huang as they become the center point of artificial intelligence.

Nvidiaâs board of directors says it is launching an independent investigation into its CEO, Jensen Huang, after reports in The New York Times and the FT revealed that Huang was likely the target of an intelligence operation run by China.

— Yashar Ali ð (@yasharali.bsky.social) 2026-11-08T20:30:12.500Z

“The NVIDIA Board is initiating an independent investigation in light of recent reporting that founder and CEO Jensen Huang has been the target of a foreign intelligence effort,” reads the company statement. “A committee of independent directors, assisted by outside independent counsel, will oversee the investigation.”

In late October, stories began to emerge that Huang was the target of a spy plot, allegedly having an affair with Megaspeed executive Alice Huang. Megaspeed, a Singapore-based company, has been under investigation since 2024 over the smuggling of Nvidia hardware into China, an increasingly common practice but breach of American sanctions nonetheless. According to the Financial Times, US intelligence alleges that the Megaspeed executive was acting directly on behalf of the Chinese government as a spy, raising concerns that sensitive intel may have been supplied on top of parts. Now having confirmed to the company that the infidelity took place, Nvidia announced that they will launch a formal investigation into their acting CEO.

“I was unfaithful to my wife,” Huang wrote in a company memo. “My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did. I am deeply sorry.”

While PC gamers are long familiar with Nvidia, the GPU makers have grown into one of the most influential companies in the world, a prime driver behind the ever growing AI sector. While AI firms still struggle to deliver a profit for themselves, Nvidia is one of the biggest benefactors of the industry, manufacturing the parts that make it possible and watching the price tag on components surge. This scarcity and federal-level AI race has also launched efforts to limit Chinaâs access to components, with Washington kicking off dramatic new policies to keep computer components and e-waste from leaving the country.

The news comes as the AI sector seemingly rankles from the results of the American midterm elections. Increased scrutiny and policy from incoming lawmakers seeming to spook the previously excitable investors. Despite the investigation, Nvidia says that Huang will remain as acting CEO. The company also maintains that despite the high scandal, they have “no reason to believe that either national security or NVIDIA secrets were compromised.”

Juventus defender Gatti added to Italy squad for Nations League

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Juventus defender Federico Gatti has been added to Roberto Mancini’s Italy squad for the Nations League matches against France and Belgium.

The Bianconeri man won’t have long to travel to the Coverciano training ground, because he is at the Stadio Artemio Franchi in Florence this evening, playing in Juve’s 2-2 Serie A draw against Fiorentina.

Mancini had originally called up Udinese’s 18-year-old Matteo Palma to replace injured Roma defender Gianluca Mancini, but he would seem to want a more experienced figure to bolster his options for these crucial matches.

Gatti is 28 years old and has eight senior caps for the Italy squad, the most recent being in the ill-fated World Cup play-off defeat on penalties to Bosnia and Herzegovina on March 31, 2026.

The Juve centre-back scored one goal with one assist in nine appearances this season between Serie A and the Europa League.

The Nations League Group A fixtures conclude with Italy hosting France at San Siro on Thursday November 12, then visiting Belgium in Brussels on Sunday November 15.

Michael Carrick sack news LIVE: Man Utd latest as meeting booked to discuss manager

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Manchester United chiefs face a huge decision to make over the future of manager Michael Carrick, who had two games to save his job and has overseen two calamitous performances and two remarkable fightbacks. Sunday’s thrilling 4-4 draw with Aston Villa was dismal on the whole, but the players showed they are up for the fight.

The pressure has been mounting on Carrick following a disappointing start to the season, and the situation took a turn for the worse this weekend as, without kicking a ball, Man Utd slipped into the Premier League relegation zone after Nottingham Forest beat Manchester City.

Disaster starts to both the first half and second half against Aston Villa appeared to leave Carrick on the brink of the sack, but Patrick Dorgu’s 96th-minute equaliser could give club chiefs something to ponder heading into the international break. Follow all the latest Carrick sack news below!

A big day ahead for Michael Carrick and Manchester United.

We will be covering all the breaking news right here, so stick with us throughout Monday and the rest of the week.

Manchester United will hold a meeting at Carrington early next week to discuss Michael Carrick's future.

Acccording to TEAMtalk, director of football Jason Wilcox will hold the meeting and deciding Carrick’s fate will be top of the agenda.

United have assessed potential replacements, including Eddie Howe, Julian Nagelsmann, Antonio Conte and Unai Emery.

Wilcox is understood to have significant influence over the outcome, having previously backed Carrick.

The Athletic’s David Ornstein is unsure if Michael Carrick will be sacked by Manchester United.

Ornstein said on NBC Sports, before today’s game: “I think the truth here at Old Trafford is that they do want Michael Carrick to succeed.

“If they wanted to remove him and make a change, I think they would’ve already done it by now and found an interim solution.

“And with that result in midweek against Roma, there was a bit of improved spirit, and let’s see what happens against Villa today.

“Think the truth is, there isn’t a firm plan here at Old Trafford for what to do if they were to make a change.

“No doubt they would’ve had conversations with representatives in recent weeks, and they’ll have more time to do so over the international break.

“A disastrous result today and maybe you think there will be a change, but as things stand, they’re continuing with Michael Carrick.

“I don’t know of a clear alternative plan.”

Manchester United legend has been on pundit duty for NBC Sports.

"Michael Carrick did that last season - so you can paper over the cracks and get a new manager bounce,” Neville told NBC Sports. "When the new manager comes in, everybody's happy for a couple of months and all of a sudden, six months later, it happens again.

"You can keep changing the manager and thinking that's going to be the answer. But this has been proven now over eight managers in 12 years, that's not the answer.

"Michael could leave, Michael might stay, we don't know, the ownership don't have a clue what to do.

"It's rinse and repeat. Amorim, Ten Hag, Ole Gunnar Solskjaer, Carrick, Jose Mourinho, Louis van Gaal, David Moyes, Ralf Rangnick - some of them are world class managers who have had success, there are a lot of promising young managers there, ex players who know the club as well.

"None of it has worked because if you recruit badly, if you've got poor performance department, poor recruitment department, you've not got leadership that is strong right through the spine of the football club you will fail.

“Every organisation in the world will fail if you've not got an aligned leadership from top to bottom - your board and your ownership right the way through to the person who is stood on the pitch trying to play football.

"There are real issues within that football club, you've got people doing those jobs who have never done those jobs before in senior positions."

Big movement in the betting markets, as Mauricio Pochettino is stormed into favouritism to replace Michael Carrick.

Michael Carrick was asked directly by Sky Sports if he expects to have talks over his future.

Sky Sports: What do you expect to happen? Clearly there’s going to be conversation?

Carrick: “Would you? ... You’re telling me, what do you think?”

Sky Sports: I’m asking you, Michael. What do you think?

Carrick: “You know the situation, we haven’t won enough games.

“But I expect to move forward with things we’ve been working on, showing a lot of the good from today tighten up a few mistakes and win some football matches.”

As per reports, Manchester United's football operations leadership, chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox hold all the power with regards to Carrick’s future.

The pair were both in attendance at Stamford Bridge last weekend and at Old Trafford on Tuesday, amid reports of being involved in discussions over potential replacements.

Taking into account that Carrick held crisis talks with Berrada and Wilcox after the Bournemouth game, they would all - along with minority shareholder Sir Jim Ratcliffe - be expected to meet at Carrington following the Villa fixture.

Michael Carrick has been speaking to BBC Sport.

“[We’re] making it hard for ourselves. Again, I thought a lot of the performances were very good. We made it challenging for ourselves. Mistakes that the boss knows shouldn't happen.

“We built our way back into the game and played well, we probably should score more than we did today. The goals are coming from individual mistakes. We need to tighten up.

"I thought we were the better team. We played some very good football and got back into the game. Two goals again put us on the back foot. In the end, we could have even won it.

“[At the end I thought] if we get one, then anything can happen. We never gave up and never stopped believing.

Roy Keane has sent a message to Manchester United fans after Sunday's 4-4 draw with Aston Villa left the club in the Premier League relegation zone.

On the pitch, Man Utd found themselves 2-0 down after four minutes, and then 4-2 down before the hour mark, but the players were applauded off by some on Sunday after a thrilling fightback.

Whether the point is enough for Carrick to keep his job remains to be seen, with Man Utd languishing in 18th place in the standings.

But the main focus of the supporters' ire was the Glazers, co-owner Sir Jim Ratcliffe and director of football Jason Wilcox.

Speaking after the game, Keane acknowledged and praised the fans.

"Manchester United fans get criticised a lot, but I think United fans are really fair supporters, I really do," he said on Sky Sports.

"They're fair with the players. We saw at the Bournemouth game the players were getting booed off. They gave them a round of applause here because the players gave a little bit of something back.

"That's the minimum you should be giving the supporters.

It’s a two-horse race between Crystal Palace’s Pierre Sage and Man Utd’s Michael Carrick to see who gets sacked first.

CEO Mike Bilder doesn't see AI as having much to offer Jackbox.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

On a recent visit to Jackbox Games HQ here in Chicago, I got to speak with developers and studio leadership about their process, history, and place in the industry. Given, you know, everything, I'm pretty much contractually obligated to ask about AI in conversations like this. Here's what CEO Mike Bilder had to say on the topic:

"The people are what make this product. The comedy, the writing, and the art, all that is done by people, and we have no intention of changing that. I don't think we're experts on what's going on with AI in the industry and any of that. There's obviously uses for tooling and for research, and [for] all that kind of stuff [AI is] incredibly powerful.

"But from a generative asset standpoint, we don't have any intention of replacing people, or people-made editorial and art with it."

From everything we had discussed up to this point, as well as Jackbox's whole deal, I didn't think generative AI would really be in the cards for the studio. But I still appreciated Bilder's straightforward, clear answer on the subject, particularly when the standard playbook these days is fence sitting reminiscent of The Simpsons' "Abortions for some, miniature American flags for others."

Jackbox's games are relatively lightweight from a tech perspective, while the art and writing is their main draw⁠—if anything was noticeably AI-generated, it would put the value of the whole package into question.

The company recently released the Jackbox Party Pack 12 on October 14, and the studio's production values have really progressed since the last entry I had played⁠—Trivia Murder Party 3 makes extensive use of realtime 3D graphics, for example. Jackbox Party Pack 12 is available on pretty much everything, but this is Personal Computer Gamer, so I'll say you can grab it on Steam.

Javier Hernandez’s Passionate Man Utd Speech Goes Viral

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

With higher-ups Jason Wilcox and Omar Berrada under just as much pressure as Michael Carrick at Manchester United fans are convinced his former teammate Javier Hernandez is worthy of becoming a board member.

United rescued a point right at the death on Sunday after Patrick Dorgu headed home a crucial equaliser that may have saved Carrick's job. His side drew 4-4 against Aston Villa at Old Trafford and he has maintained that he is looking forward to rather than contemplating whether he'll still be in charge beyond that game.

Carrick's men produced another comeback just days after a remarkable turnaround against AS Roma, coming from behind to beat the Italians 3-2. Many of the club's legends have had plenty to say about the dire situation they find themselves in, sitting in the relegation zone in November.

Hernandez never experienced such tough times during his United career, instead winning two Premier League titles under Alex Ferguson. The Mexican striker still plays his football at the age of 38 for US outfit Atlantic Dallas but his motivational pre-match talk with Sky Sports hints at a future job in management or as a director.

After sharing the pitch with Carrick throughout his spell at Old Trafford, he gave an interesting perspective on the current state of the club. He explained why he is backing his former teammate to turn things around:

"I am confident, I am a believer that sometimes these decisions cannot be in your own way, I'm not saying that because I am in completely favour, I'm saying it in the world. I am more in favour of if a decision is taken already, instead of staying and complaining, go with it and let's see if it was right or not."

Hernandez recalled Carrick's success as interim coach: "Last season he did an amazing job, he brought United back to the Champions League. Now the start of the season wasn't like what we expected, we are used to normally when you have a really good season to maintain that, but football is like that."

Delving into the United boss' personality, he spoke highly of the characteristics that make Carrick a good fit: "I think Michael is the correct man. His personality helps a lot because he has a lot of composure and he's a very calm guy. I think he's the correct guy."

Hernandez particularly gave an inspiring assessment of what is going wrong and how the players need to take more responsibility for their own careers:

"The players need to perform over there, after this white line, when the whistle blows, there's nothing much more the manager can do. They can tell you defend more, run more, but putting on this Manchester United shirt, you shouldn't be looking at what is lacking, you need to be giving your life for this club. It can be one of your best or the best chapter in your career."

Hernandez spent five years at United and quickly became a fan favourite with the nickname 'Chicarito'. A cult hero, he scored 59 goals in 157 games and clearly still has a lot of affection for the club, disappointed to see Carrick's players not giving their all for the Old Trafford outfit.

United fans liked what they heard from Hernandez and have talked him up as a possible candidate for a high-ranking role with the club. That probably isn't something under his consideration right now given he's still playing out in the United States.

One fan pointed out his passion compared to today's coaches: "Chicharito delivered more fire through television than some managers."

Another supporter wants the Mexican to return: "Bring chicha back to united as an manager assistant or to the board!!"

One suggested he takes up a position upstairs: "Put him in that MUnited Board!"

Another feels he embodies what United is about: "ChichaGoat should be involved in the board, his mentality matches this club's spirit."

One added: "Roy Keane would love this lad."

While most of the talk following the draw against Villa is about Carrick and his future as United head coach, there is growing discontent in the fanbase about co-owners INEOS. Particularly sporting director Wilcox and CEO Berrada whose recruitment and direction for the club have raised alarm bells.

Michael Carrick watched another United car crash.

Putting a former player in a board member position is an idea that has been floated throughout the post-Sir Alex Ferguson era. There had been talk of Sheikh Jassim wanting the Class of 1992 to be part of his ownership of the club when he rivaled INEOS in a takeover race.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Free GHA Titanium Hotel Status For Bank Of America’s $100,000 Rewards Tier

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

If you have $100,000 in qualifying Bank of America and Merrill balances, check your banking rewards before paying for hotel elite status. There’s a reported benefit tucked inside the program that gets you GHA Discovery Titanium, with no hotel stay required. On the November 6 Frequent Miler on the Air episode, Greg Davis-Kean said he used this himself. His status upgrade was instant and runs through December 31, 2027. I’ve called GHA my second favorite hotel loyalty program, after Hyatt. I’ve received real benefits at hotels I actually wanted to stay in. Where To Find The Bank Of America Offer Greg reports that it works for Preferred Honors and Premier members, as a one-time match. That’s based on banking relationship tier rather than specific card product. Bank of America publishes the requirements for access to its lifestyle benefits: Preferred Honors requires an eligible checking account and a combined three-month average daily balance of at least $100,000 in qualifying Bank of America deposits and/or Merrill investment accounts. Premier generally starts at $1 million. Bank of America Private Bank clients can qualify for Premier regardless of balances. Enrolled members can find lifestyle benefits under the Rewards tab in online banking or MyMerrill, or through the lifestyle benefits tile in BofA Rewards. Look in the travel offers for GHA Discovery and follow the specific activation link. The $100,000 doesn’t have to be cash in a checking account, it includes investments at Merrill count. The Value Of GHA Titanium Status GHA Discovery is the loyalty program for roughly 1,000 hotels across about 60 brands, including Kempinski, Anantara, Capella, Pan Pacific, NH and Lore Group. It’s a way for smaller hotel groups to offer recognition and rewards across a much larger collection. I covered the program’s relaunch in December 2021, when it added Discovery Dollars (essentially, points-earning, it had been primarily a recognition program before that) and made status easier to earn. Titanium is the highest published tier. Normally, Titanium takes 30 eligible nights, $15,000 in eligible spending, or stays at three qualifying brands in a calendar year. The main Titanium benefits are: A two-category room upgrade, subject to availability, and that can be a suite. 11 a.m. early check-in and 4 p.m. late checkout, both subject to availability. Request early check-in at least two days ahead. A welcome amenity. 7% back in Discovery Dollars on eligible spending. Each dollar is worth one U.S. dollar towards eligible hotel charges, and regularly earned Titanium dollars expire after 24 months. There’s also breakfast for two at participating brands on direct bookings. This has expanded since I covered the benefit’s introduction in 2024, but it still isn’t universal. Lore Group and Capella participate, as do Anantara and many others. Titanium doesn’t guarantee club lounge access. And the ability to gift Titanium to someone else requires earning the status through the normal qualifying criteria so a match alone doesn’t get you that benefit. I’ve made several GHA Discovery stays through Amex Fine Hotels and Resorts as well as Chase’s The Edit, and benefits stack. At NH Collection Palazzo dei Dogi in Venice, early check-in and 4 p.m. checkout was especially useful. At Riggs in Washington, D.C., I booked through Chase’s The Edit and combined its benefits with GHA status. My suite was the former bank boardroom, with a dining table for twelve. I also combined American Express Fine Hotels + Resorts with GHA at Kempinski Cancun, receiving an oceanfront suite and club access. Greg’s Augustine Prague story was a good experience as well. He’d booked a hotel that converted to a Kempinski so he added his new GHA number and contacted the hotel. They combined the Fine Hotels + Resorts upgrade with Titanium’s double upgrade and put him in a historic Treasury suite. He also earned D$75 from the stay. Don’t Have The Bank Relationship? There’s Still A Paid Match I’ve covered GHA’s unusually broad status match offers before, including eligibility through airline and cruise status and selected credit cards rather than just another hotel program. The current paid match is scheduled to run through November 30, 2026, with status through December 31, 2027: Titanium costs $150 and comes with D$75. Platinum costs $100 and comes with D$50. The gift dollars expire after 12 months, and there isn’t a stay challenge required. Qualifying Titanium matches include Hilton Diamond, Hyatt Globalist, Marriott Platinum and above, and IHG Platinum and above, among others. For someone who will use all D$75, the Titanium offer has a $75 net cost. You can potentially match GHA Titanium onward to other programs as well. And the easiest way to qualify in some cities would be three one night stays at different brands. Topics on this pageBank of AmericaGHA DiscoveryLoyalty programAmerican ExpressChaseGlobal Hotel AllianceHyattKempinskiKempinski CancunPan Pacific Hotels and ResortsTitaniumVeniceWashington+10 more More From View from the Wing

They combined the Fine Hotels + Resorts upgrade with Titanium’s double upgrade and put him in a historic Treasury suite. He also earned D$75 from the stay. Don’t Have The Bank Relationship? There’s Still A Paid Match I’ve covered GHA’s unusually broad status match offers before, including eligibility through airline and cruise status and selected credit cards rather than just another hotel program. The current paid match is scheduled to run through November 30, 2026, with status through December 31, 2027: Titanium costs $150 and comes with D$75. Platinum costs $100 and comes with D$50. The gift dollars expire after 12 months, and there isn’t a stay challenge required. Qualifying Titanium matches include Hilton Diamond, Hyatt Globalist, Marriott Platinum and above, and IHG Platinum and above, among others. For someone who will use all D$75, the Titanium offer has a $75 net cost. You can potentially match GHA Titanium onward to other programs as well. And the easiest way to qualify in some cities would be three one night stays at different brands. Greg reports that it works for Preferred Honors and Premier members, as a one-time match. That’s based on banking relationship tier rather than specific card product. Bank of America publishes the requirements for access to its lifestyle benefits:

Look in the travel offers for GHA Discovery and follow the specific activation link. The $100,000 doesn’t have to be cash in a checking account, it includes investments at Merrill count.

GHA Discovery is the loyalty program for roughly 1,000 hotels across about 60 brands, including Kempinski, Anantara, Capella, Pan Pacific, NH and Lore Group. It’s a way for smaller hotel groups to offer recognition and rewards across a much larger collection. I covered the program’s relaunch in December 2021, when it added Discovery Dollars (essentially, points-earning, it had been primarily a recognition program before that) and made status easier to earn. Titanium is the highest published tier. Normally, Titanium takes 30 eligible nights, $15,000 in eligible spending, or stays at three qualifying brands in a calendar year. The main Titanium benefits are: A two-category room upgrade, subject to availability, and that can be a suite. 11 a.m. early check-in and 4 p.m. late checkout, both subject to availability. Request early check-in at least two days ahead. A welcome amenity. 7% back in Discovery Dollars on eligible spending. Each dollar is worth one U.S. dollar towards eligible hotel charges, and regularly earned Titanium dollars expire after 24 months. There’s also breakfast for two at participating brands on direct bookings. This has expanded since I covered the benefit’s introduction in 2024, but it still isn’t universal. Lore Group and Capella participate, as do Anantara and many others. Titanium doesn’t guarantee club lounge access. And the ability to gift Titanium to someone else requires earning the status through the normal qualifying criteria so a match alone doesn’t get you that benefit. I’ve made several GHA Discovery stays through Amex Fine Hotels and Resorts as well as Chase’s The Edit, and benefits stack. At NH Collection Palazzo dei Dogi in Venice, early check-in and 4 p.m. checkout was especially useful. At Riggs in Washington, D.C., I booked through Chase’s The Edit and combined its benefits with GHA status. My suite was the former bank boardroom, with a dining table for twelve. I also combined American Express Fine Hotels + Resorts with GHA at Kempinski Cancun, receiving an oceanfront suite and club access. Greg’s Augustine Prague story was a good experience as well. He’d booked a hotel that converted to a Kempinski so he added his new GHA number and contacted the hotel. Nick Jonas Admits He 'Resents' His Diabetes Diagnosis in Candid Open Letter

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Nick Jonas is reflecting on navigating diabetes more than two decades after receiving his diagnosis.

“It’s funny how often we talk,” Jonas, 34, wrote in an open letter as part of Dexcom’s A Letter to Diabetes initiative. “Staring at my phone to see where my glucose is. Reaching for my pump to bolus because I’m about to eat something with 17 carbs. Those are just a couple of the hundreds of little decisions I make every week.”

He continued, “Most people never notice them. But they add up to a real relationship, because that’s what diabetes is. It’s an ongoing conversation between your body, your mind and your devices.”

The Jonas Brothers star, who is now a father, shared that the “unpredictability” of diabetes “threw” him when he initially received his diagnosis in 2005 at the age of 13. (Jonas and wife Priyanka Chopra Jonas welcomed their daughter, Malti, via surrogate in 2022.)

“I’d built my whole life around this unrealistic structure and belief that I was in control. You took that apart over the years, bit by bit,” he wrote. “Part of me is grateful I learned that lesson. Part of me resents you for it. Full stop. There are days I wonder what life would feel like without you.”

Despite resenting the disease for its impact on his life, Jonas also reflected on the positives of learning to live with Type 1 diabetes.

“I always struggled to feel like I belonged anywhere. At school, I felt misunderstood. In most circles, for that matter. Through my childhood and youth, that was the theme. It wasn’t until I started talking openly about life with type 1, and meeting other people who were living it too, that I finally felt like I belonged somewhere,” he explained, adding that he never wanted to be seen as a “victim.”

From Jonas’ perspective, his candidness about this journey may help others see someone like him “out there living their dreams.”

“I share my story for the 13-year-old who just got diagnosed and is lying in a hospital bed, scared to death. I want them to be able to look up and see someone out there living their dreams while managing all of this, every single day,” he said. “So thank you, old friend, for the lessons I never asked for. I’m going to keep sharing my story and telling the truth, so someone out there feels a little less alone.”

Assessing if Avalanche’s growing network activity can spur AVAX’s price recovery

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Avalanche sits among the top-30 cryptos by market cap with a value of close to $3.64 billion. It’s in the news today after it crossed the 1.60 billion mark for total transactions on the mainnet since launching in September 2020. This new milestone was achieved despite a bearish crypto market.

However, that’s not the entire story. Especially since the three-month average for transactions seemed to be far below the volume it commanded in its initial days.

At press time, the number was well below 1 million, while the volume 6 years ago when it launched was above 3 million.

Worth noting, however, that in the last 24 hours, its network fees spiked by 30%, indicating network congestion. Hence, the question: What’s behind the uptick in the number of transactions?

On-chain data from CoinGlass revealed that AVAX traders have been defying the market downtrend. Both Spot and Futures markets were net buying at 51.60%, with a bullish divergence confirmed on the 15-minute chart.

The Futures CVD was at $2.39 million, while that of Spot was at $462K. This implied that the derivatives market was more optimistic than the spot one.

These traders could be buying the dip since AVAX has been on a downtrend over the past week. In fact, it fell from around $12 to $8, a loss equivalent to about 32%.

At press time, its mindshare was also down 10%, with sentiment from top influencers turning neutral.

The existing optimism could be attributed to the ecosystem growth, especially on the real-world front. It’s growing exponentially on a quarter-on-quarter basis, especially when the third and second quarters were compared.

For instance, Avalanche’s DEX volume doubled to about $5.33 billion, indicating heightened trading activity both on buys and sells.

Additionally, RWAs on C-Chain reached $1.22 billion, with the tokenized treasuries leading with a growth of 14% to reach $545 million. At the same time, Dinari stock purchases jumped by over 34%, equivalent to $9 million. These figures confirmed AVAX’s growing RWA financial activity.

Finally, card spending surged by 74%, which totaled more than $10 million. Similarly, on-chain credit grew by 34%, reaching $37.40 million. Both implied that traders have been using Avalanche for payments.

Despite such strong activity, if the higher high at $7.90 is lost as support, AVAX could slide to $6.85 or subsequently test the psychological level at $6. However, if capital flows into the chain through RWA, the downside risk could be managed.

Muriuki Lazaro is a on-chain data analyst with a B.Sc. in Data Science. Muriuki specializes in dissecting complex on-chain data into clear and accurate insights for readers in the crypto ecosystem, with a particular focus on Bitcoin.

'Zlatan Ibrahimovic Ended My England Career in 10 Minutes - I Never Played Again'

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

While plenty of attention is devoted to Zlatan Ibrahimovic's character and his larger-than-life personality, his ego wouldn't have been nearly as effective if he hadn't also been one of the best players on the planet during the prime of his career. The Swede was a phenomenon in front of goal, ranking among the greatest strikers in football history, and wherever he played, he rarely struggled to find the back of the net.

Ibrahimovic represented some of the biggest teams in world football throughout his career and ranks among the most decorated footballers of all time when it comes to trophies won. He played for Barcelona, Inter Milan, AC Milan, Paris Saint-Germain and Manchester United, alongside several other notable clubs. His imposing frame was matched by remarkable agility and speed, meaning there were few defenders in the beautiful game capable of keeping him quiet for an entire match. Having retired in 2023, the striker finished with 949 appearances for club and country, scoring 558 goals in the process.

Facing the superstar during his prime could have serious consequences for a player's career too, as one England centre-back discovered when his international career was effectively brought to an end after going up against Ibrahimovic.

Zlatan Ibrahimovic says his former Manchester United teammate deserves credit for Cristiano Ronaldo’s success at Old Trafford.

After several seasons impressing for Stoke City in the Premier League, Ryan Shawcross received his first-ever call-up to the England team in 2012. Supporters had been pushing for the defender to get an opportunity with the national side for some time and he was finally handed his chance, but his debut came against Sweden. He was tasked with trying to deal with Ibrahimovic and, by his own admission, things didn't go according to plan. In fact, the incident turned him into a source of jokes within his family. Via quotes shared by the Telegraph, Shawcross said:

"I’m the butt of the joke in my family, just because the 10 minutes I had in international football was ruined by one man. They are consistently saying that Zlatan ruined my career! I had a chance and I don’t think many other defenders have stopped him. I was given 10 minutes to show what I could do and probably didn’t provide a good enough performance. I was against one of the best strikers in the world who was on a hot streak at the time and these things happen."

The centre-back was able to look back on the episode with good humour at the time, but he never earned another England appearance for the rest of his career. That was unfortunate, considering he enjoyed a strong spell in the Premier League and had arguably done enough to deserve another opportunity.

Zlatan Ibrahimovic previously had tattoos on his body that are no longer there - here's the story behind it.

He never managed to join one of England's biggest clubs, but for much of his career, Shawcross was a top-level defender who held his own against some of the Premier League's finest strikers. After progressing through the Manchester United academy, he spent time on loan with Royal Antwerp and Stoke before eventually making his move to the latter permanent. The Potters were playing in the Championship at the time, but they soon secured promotion to the top flight with Shawcross at the centre of their defence.

From there, he helped Stoke establish themselves as a midtable Premier League side, with the club remaining in the top flight for 10 seasons before eventually being relegated to the Championship. Throughout his Premier League spell, Shawcross was highly impressive and attracted interest from several clubs over the years. No move ultimately materialised, however, and he stayed with Stoke for 13 years.

Across that period, the centre-back made 453 appearances for the Potters, including 317 in the top flight. He was a hugely important servant to the club, but departed in 2021 to join Inter Miami. His stay there lasted just one season before he retired, and he never represented England again. It's fair to say that Ibrahimovic may have played a role in that.
